  | any other `route.ts` exporting POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE | — | None exists in Phase 1. The test fails until the new route is added to the table with its wrapper and test (a later phase decides, e.g. `linkAction()` for client link routes, §9 AUTH-01 ruling). |

  Not HTTP entry points, recorded in the same test file as a list the test does not walk: pg-boss job handlers (`apps/web/src/jobs/*`; they run in the worker under `withWorkspace(data.workspaceId)` and re-check membership where they act for a user, Task 27 Step 2; EVT rules), the CLI scripts `db:bootstrap` and `jobs:retry` (operator commands with admin or `crm_worker` credentials, never reachable over HTTP; runbooks).

### Task 31: `auth_audit_log`: login sessions and 2FA events, one row per event per user (ASK 3 = B)

**How this task is decided (owner scope choice B, Charlie 2026-10-21; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-21).** Eight Bob plan review rounds each found a new timing issue in how Better Auth 1.7.6 does login and 2FA (several separate autocommit statements per flow, fact iii), and each paper fix added more triggers. This task is therefore split into three parts, and only the first two are binding:
- **31.A Requirements (R1–R15)** — what must hold, each traceable to the spec, to ASK 3 = B or to a Bob finding. **Binding.**
- **31.B Required tests (T1–T15)** — how each requirement is proven, including the failure-injection points and orchestrated races Bob named. **Binding** (the spike may add tests or replace one with an equal or stronger one; it may not drop one).
- **31.C Step 0 — design spike, the first deliverable of PR 4** — the mechanism is chosen there, built against the installed Better Auth, proven with the real tests of 31.B, and reviewed by Bob as real code before the rest of PR 4 is built.

The detailed triggers, hooks, plugins, SQL and Better Auth facts written in Rounds 1–7 are kept in **31.E "Candidate designs"** below. They are **candidate designs: not reviewed to completion and not claimed sound** (Round 8 found a lockout they do not solve). The spike may keep, change or replace any of them. Bob reviews 31.A and 31.B in this plan, not the candidate mechanism.

**Prerequisite:** spec revision 8 (Task 30, edit E12) approved, merged and reconciled. The PR order already requires this before PR 1 starts. E12 states the requirements, not the mechanism. If Bob's review of revision 8 changes E12, this task follows the approved text, and the plan is corrected before PR 4 starts.

**Spec basis** (line numbers for hash `50acbcfe…`; E12 adds the table to each): "Audit, logging, privacy" line 835 ("Audit log append-only; records logins, 2FA, …"; personal-data fields stored as `"[pii]"`; "entries reference IDs"); data model line 489 (`audit_log` columns, including `ip`: the pattern this table follows, minus the workspace); Tenancy lines 781–782 (no-RLS tables are reached only through the system module; the no-RLS allow-list test); "Authentication and sessions" line 748 ("All sessions revoked on password, 2FA or email change": AUTH-07); "Better Auth endpoints" line 747 (Phase 1 enables sign-in, session list and revoke, change password, and "TOTP 2FA enrol, verify, disable and recovery codes").

#### 31.A Requirements (binding)

"Login" below means a completed sign-in that leaves the user with a usable session (after the second factor when 2FA is on). "Login row" means the one audit row that records it (in the candidate vocabulary: `session_issued` with reason `sign_in`; the names are open point B). "2FA change" means exactly three actions, and only these end the user's other sessions (R5, AUTH-07): **enabling 2FA** (the TOTP confirmation that turns it on), **disabling 2FA**, and **regenerating the recovery codes**. **Consuming a recovery code to sign in is not a 2FA change:** it is a login method (the second step of a 2FA sign-in, `method` `backup_code`). It is audited atomically as that login (R2, R3) and as a recovery-code change event (R4: in the candidate vocabulary `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`), but it ends no other session, because revoking the user's other devices each time a code is used would sign users out of their own devices whenever they use the fallback they were given (for example, on a new phone). This scope is a builder proposal and a security-policy choice: open point F (Bob rules; Charlie decides if Bob or Charlie wants consumption to revoke too). R1, R4, R5, T1, T4, T5 and E12 (a), (d), (f) use this one list. (Bob plan review Round 9, P2.)

| # | Requirement | Source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Every login, every 2FA change and every recovery-code consumption is recorded in `auth_audit_log`, one row per event, keyed by user, with no workspace: also for a user with no workspace, and once (not per workspace) for a user in several. | spec line 835; ASK 3 = B (Charlie 2026-10-20) |
| R2 | **No session exists without its audit row.** The row is written atomically with the session: if the audit write fails, the session is not stored and no token leaves the server (no cookie, no `token` in a body); a process or connection death at any point leaves either both or neither. | line 835; Bob plan review Rounds 4 and 5 (P1) |
| R3 | **Every login is exactly one login row.** A failed attempt (wrong password, wrong code) writes none; a token that is not a login (the password step of a 2FA sign-in, a rotation) is recorded as what it is, never as a login. | line 835; Bob Rounds 3 and 6 |
| R4 | **A 2FA change, and a recovery-code consumption, records its event atomically with the change.** A failed audit write leaves 2FA exactly as it was (flag, `two_factor` row, codes, sessions); no committed 2FA change and no consumed code lacks its row. | line 835; Bob Rounds 4 and 5 |
| R5 | **Each 2FA change ends the user's other sessions: enabling 2FA, disabling 2FA and regenerating the recovery codes** (the device that made the change keeps its session: a rotated one after enable and disable, its existing one after regeneration). **Consuming a recovery code to sign in ends no other session** (a login method, not a 2FA change; definition above, open point F). A successful response to a 2FA change means the other sessions are gone: if ending them fails, the request does not report success (R11 still applies to enable and disable). For regeneration, the new codes and the end of the other sessions commit together or not at all: a failure leaves the old codes valid and the sessions as they were, so a failed request never leaves the user with codes they were not shown. Stock 1.7.6 does none of this itself (candidate fact xiv; `/two-factor/generate-backup-codes` is one `two_factor` update and no session call, `backup-codes/index.mjs` lines 277–304, read 2026-10-22), so Phase 1 must. | AUTH-07, spec line 748; Bob Round 7; Bob Round 9 (P2) |
| R6 | **No password-only session survives a completed enable,** including a sign-in that runs concurrently with the enable, in both orders (sign-in stored first, enable committed first). The mirror holds for disable: no session whose 2FA decision predates a committed disable is issued with a stale decision. | AUTH-07, line 748; Bob Round 7 (P1) |
| R7 | **A concurrent 2FA change and a sign-in never produce an audit reason that disagrees with the result:** a completed login has exactly one login row, a challenge has none, in both directions (0→1, 1→0). | line 835; Bob Round 6 (P1) |
| R8 | **A trusted-device cookie minted before Phase 1 cannot skip the 2FA challenge.** | line 747 (trusted devices are not a Phase 1 feature); Bob Round 6 (P1) |
| R9 | **`trustDevice: true` is refused,** and no new trusted-device record or cookie can be created. | line 747; Bob Round 6 |
| R10 | **`/list-sessions` never returns session tokens** (any field outside a display allow-list), on every entry path (`/api/auth/*`, `postToAuth`, server-side `auth.api`). | line 835 (no token reachable for an unaudited or failed login); Bob Rounds 4 and 5 |
| R11 | **No lockout from 2FA enrolment (NEW, Bob plan review Round 8, P1).** No interruption or failure at **any** step of 2FA enrolment may leave the user locked out: not after the flag commits and before `two_factor.verified = true` is written, not when the replacement-session insert fails, not when any audit write fails, not on a process or connection death between any two of Better Auth's statements. The user must be able to complete enrolment, or safely restart it, **without an operator reset**. The acceptable recovery policy is open point A (options listed there; none is picked here, and a weaker policy is not final without Charlie). The same is checked for the steps of disable (builder addition; Bob confirms in open point B). | AUTH-07 together with line 747 (enrol must work); Bob Round 8 (P1) |
| R12 | **Append-only, no reader, system module only.** No runtime role or PUBLIC holds SELECT, UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE on `auth_audit_log`; `crm_app` holds at most INSERT; no RLS, on the no-RLS allow-list, reached only through the system module (TEN-10 lint). | E12; TEN-03, TEN-10, UNI-10; ASK 3 = B |
| R13 | **No personal data or secret in a row or in an error log:** no email, name, password, TOTP code, recovery code, TOTP secret, session token or user agent; `detail` holds IDs and fixed labels only. | line 835; UNI-09 |
| R14 | **No new privilege and no off switch for runtime roles.** The mechanism adds no privilege beyond those stated in the manifest delta of the spike (at most `crm_app` INSERT on `auth_audit_log`), and no runtime role can disable, replace or bypass it. | TEN-02 (hard), TEN-03 |
| R15 | **Cutover from a Phase 0 database: every pre-existing session is invalidated (NEW, Bob plan review Round 9, P1).** Phase 0 already stores live sessions (`apps/web/drizzle/0000_auth_baseline.sql`, table `session`), and none has an audit row. Policy (builder proposal; open point G): the migration that makes the audit mechanism live (candidate numbering 0017; whichever migration the spike makes the one that installs the mechanism) **deletes every `session` row first, in the same migration and the same transaction, before the mechanism's objects are created**, and before that delete it locks `session` against concurrent writes (`LOCK TABLE public.session IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE` or stronger, held to commit) so that a session inserted by a still-running Phase 0 process can neither slip in between the delete and the mechanism going live nor commit without its row. **No historical login row is fabricated:** the migration writes no `auth_audit_log` row. So when the migrations finish, no usable session exists without its audit row, and R2 (the session invariant) holds from the first request Phase 1 serves. Every Phase 0 session token stops working at cutover. **User effect:** everyone signs in again once after the upgrade. Phase 0 holds only test data, and the dev database is reset anyway by the Phase 1 role change (Task 2 Step 6, Risk 5); for a later production upgrade from a Phase 0 database the same rule applies and is announced beforehand in the upgrade notes and the runbook ("all users will be signed out once"). **Alternative, rejected for now:** keep the Phase 0 sessions and record each as `session_issued` with a new reason `pre_existing` and honest provenance (`created_at` = the migration time, the session's own `created_at` in `detail`, never a `sign_in` row). It keeps users signed in, but it needs a new event category in the closed list and a change to E12 (a) and (d), it records logins nobody observed, and Phase 0 has no real users to keep signed in. **Order with migration 0018 (trusted devices):** sessions first. The cutover belongs to the migration that installs the session mechanism, because that is the moment R2 starts to bind; 0018 (candidate) runs after it in the same `migrate` run, unchanged, and both complete before `web` serves (the `migrate` service runs before `web` and `worker`). Merging them is not needed: a trust-device row is not a session, and any session obtained with an old trust cookie after the cutover goes through the audited insert path (and through R8). Keeping 0018 separate also keeps T8's `stopBefore: "0018"` instance meaningful. | R2; line 835; UNI-15; Bob Round 9 (P1) |

#### 31.B Required tests (binding)

All in `apps/web/src/lib/auth/auth-audit.test.ts` (Testcontainers, the real `crm_app` role, a real Better Auth 1.7.6 instance built by `createAuth`) unless named otherwise; catalog tests in `privileges.test.ts`, `rls.test.ts` and `lint-rules.test.ts`. State is read through `adminUrl`. **The session invariant** = every `session` row has its audit row (asserted in `afterEach`). Where a candidate test in 31.E (Candidate step 2 and Candidate step 4) already specifies a case, its exact assertions are the starting point; the names in brackets are those cases.

| Req. | Required tests |
|---|---|
| T1 (R1) | ["user with no workspace"] 1 row after sign-in; ["user in two workspaces"] 1 row and no `workspace_id` column; one row per 2FA change ["2FA enable", "2FA disable", "recovery codes" (regeneration)] and the rows of a recovery-code sign-in (the login row and the recovery-code change row) ["recovery codes" (consumption)]; ["both entry paths"] the same through `signInAction`. |
| T2 (R2) | **Forced audit failure at each session-creating step:** password sign-in, password step of a 2FA user, second step (TOTP and recovery code), rotation on enable, rotation on disable, rotation on password change → no session row, no non-empty session cookie, no `token`, the session invariant holds ["forced audit-write failure: no session exists without its row"]; an existing session cannot list or use the failed login's token; **interruption:** a backend killed inside the session-creating statement leaves neither row, and a run without the kill leaves both ["interruption inside the statement"]; ["the password-step session survives a failed Better Auth delete"] (the residual, open point C). |
| T3 (R3) | Exactly one login row per completed login on every path (password; password + TOTP; password + recovery code) ["2FA sign-in"]; zero for each failure ["failures write nothing"]; zero for non-login writes ["no double rows"]; no row with an unknown reason ["every Phase 1 path is labelled"]. |
| T4 (R4) | **Forced audit failure at each 2FA change and at a recovery-code consumption:** enable, disable, regeneration, recovery-code consumption → the request fails; flag, `two_factor` row (`verified`, `secret`, `backup_codes` byte-equal) and sessions are as before; 0 rows; after the grant is restored the account behaves as its unchanged state says ["forced audit-write failure: the 2FA change is rolled back"]. |
| T5 (R5) | **Every included action, each with a second active session C of the same user** (signed in on another "device" before the action): (a) **enable** (TOTP confirmed) → C is gone (`/get-session` with C's cookie returns null, no `session` row for C's token) and only the changer's rotated session remains ["2FA enable" AUTH-07 part]; (b) **disable** → the same ["2FA disable" AUTH-07 part]; (c) **regeneration** (`/two-factor/generate-backup-codes`) → C is gone, the changer's session is kept and works, the new codes are in the body and one of them completes a later 2FA sign-in, the old codes are refused ["recovery codes" AUTH-07 part]; (c2) for regeneration, a forced failure of ending the other sessions (test-only seam) → the request fails, `backup_codes` byte-equal to the snapshot (an old code still works), C still works, no recovery-code change row. **Negative case, consumption** (B signs in on a new device with password + recovery code while C and the changer's session A are active) → the sign-in completes; A and C are **kept** and both still pass `/get-session`; exactly one login row with `method` `backup_code` and one recovery-code change row for this sign-in (written atomically, T4); the code used is refused on a second use. End to end through `next start` for (a), (b), (c) and the negative case (Task 24 Step 2). |
| T6 (R6) | **Orchestrated races, both orders and both directions:** a sign-in whose decision is taken before an enable commits and whose session would be stored after it ends with no usable password-only session; a sign-in stored before the enable is ended by it ["concurrent 2FA flag change during a sign-in" seam 2; "lock order" L1 and L2, 0→1 and 1→0, incl. the lock-timeout run]; a **control** reproduces Bob's Round 7 bypass on a build without the fix. The orchestration (spies at Better Auth's internal adapter, database fixtures that hold a statement with an advisory lock and observe `pg_stat_activity`) is test-only and may change with the design; the outcomes may not. |
| T7 (R7) | ["concurrent 2FA flag change during a sign-in"] seam 1 and seam 2, 0→1 and 1→0: number of login rows = 1 exactly when the response is a completed login; a **control** reproduces Bob's Round 6 disagreement on a build without the fix. |
| T8 (R8) | ["a trust cookie minted under Phase 0 does not skip the challenge"] migration path, asserting the old behaviour first on a Phase 0 instance; each protective layer the chosen design has, tested alone ["each trust layer alone"]. |
| T9 (R9) | ["trusted devices refused"] 400 on both verify paths with `trustDevice: true`; ["trusted-device records are refused"] if the design keeps a database refusal. |
| T10 (R10) | ["`/list-sessions` gives no token"]: first on an instance without the protection (the token is returned: not a non-problem), then on the configured instance through every entry path. |
| T11 (R11) | **Failure injection at every enrolment step (NEW, Bob Round 8).** For each point below, the test then shows the user can complete enrolment, or restart it and complete it, with only their password and authenticator, and with no admin statement; and that no state lets anyone skip a **completed** 2FA. Points: (a) process killed (or connection dropped) after the flag `UPDATE` commits and before `two_factor.verified = true`; (b) the `verified = true` write fails; (c) the replacement (rotation) session insert fails, by forced audit failure and by a non-audit error; (d) forced audit failure at each statement of the enrolment flow in turn; (e) the same four points for disable (flag cleared; `two_factor` row delete; rotation insert). Each point is set by a test-only seam (a spy on the internal adapter, or a database fixture that fails or holds one chosen statement), on its own database. A **control** reproduces Bob's Round 8 lockout on the Round 7 candidate (the revocation in the flag's statement). |
| T12 (R12) | Manifest equality (`crm_app` at most INSERT); TEN-03 append-only part 1 extended to `auth_audit_log` (no SELECT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE for any runtime role or PUBLIC; `42501` behaviourally); no-RLS allow-list (eleven tables, no policy); TEN-10 lint fixtures for `authAuditLog`. |
| T13 (R13) | ["PII"] scan of every row the file writes; captured logs of every forced failure hold no token, email, IP or user agent. |
| T14 (R14) | Catalog test of whatever the design installs (triggers, functions, hooks, plugin order): exactly the listed objects, owned by `crm_migrator`, no EXECUTE to any role, and every disable/replace/bypass attempt as `crm_app` fails `42501`. |
| T15 (R15) | ["Phase 0 sessions are invalidated at cutover"] **migration path from an actual Phase 0 database** (NEW, Bob Round 9). On its own `startDatabase({ stopBefore: "0001" })` instance (only Phase 0's `0000_auth_baseline` applied; the Phase 1 helper still bootstraps the roles, so the closest faithful Phase 0 database the harness can build), a **Phase 0 instance** (test-only: `createAuth` as Phase 0 configured it, connected as the table owner the way Phase 0's single database user was, since no Phase 1 grant exists yet) signs up user U and signs in for real twice (sessions S1 and S2; a second user V with 2FA enrolled signs in with password + TOTP, session S3); the test keeps each session cookie and asserts the old behaviour first: `/get-session` with each cookie returns its session on the Phase 0 instance, and through `adminUrl` three `session` rows exist. Then `applyRemaining()` applies **every** Phase 1 migration **without resetting the database**. Then, **before any request is made to a Phase 1 instance**, through `adminUrl`: no `session` row remains (or, if Bob rules the alternative, every remaining row has its audit row); `auth_audit_log` holds **zero rows** (no fabricated login row; nothing dated before the cutover); the session-invariant query of T2 returns no violation. Then on the **configured** Phase 1 instance: `/get-session` with each kept cookie (S1, S2, S3) returns null and sets no session cookie; `/list-sessions` with S1 is refused; the config test asserts no session cookie cache is configured (so no cookie-held session can outlive its row); U and V can sign in again normally and each new login has exactly one login row. **Concurrent Phase 0 writer:** on a second instance at `stopBefore` the cutover migration, a test-only connection runs an `INSERT INTO session` in both orders (orchestrated with an advisory lock and `pg_stat_activity`, as in T6): (1) the insert's transaction is open before the migration's lock request (the migration waits; after it commits, that session row is gone); (2) the insert starts after the lock is taken (it waits; after the migration commits it either has its audit row or has failed). In both, the session invariant holds after commit. **Order with 0018:** the same run asserts the cutover migration is ordered before 0018 in the journal, and that a Phase 0 trust-device cookie minted in the Phase 0 part of this test leaves no `trust-device-%` row after `applyRemaining()` (T8 asserted from the real Phase 0 point). A **control** runs the same Phase 0 steps with the cutover statement removed (test-only migration set) and shows the defect: S1 still returns a session and the invariant query reports it. |

#### 31.C Step 0 — design spike (first deliverable of PR 4)

- [ ] **Step 0: Branch** `feat/p1-action-audit` (Task 12 Step 0 then reuses it). Build the spike **before** Tasks 12–15.
- [ ] **Step 0.1: Re-read the facts** that any design relies on in the installed `node_modules/better-auth` and `@better-auth/core` (start from candidate facts (i)–(xiv) in 31.E; add each new fact the chosen design uses), with file and line, into the PR body.
- [ ] **Step 0.2: Build** the `auth_audit_log` table (columns in 31.E Candidate step 3; not mechanism-dependent), its grant and the chosen mechanism, against the installed Better Auth. Start from the candidate designs; change or replace any part a test shows wrong. The Round 8 lockout (31.E, "Unsolved") must be solved here. The migration that installs the mechanism also carries the R15 cutover (lock `session`, delete every row, then create the mechanism; no audit row written), whatever the mechanism is; and R5's regeneration case (which the candidate does not cover, 31.E "Why `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`") is built here.
- [ ] **Step 0.3: Prove each requirement** with the real tests T1–T15 (write each test first and see it fail for the right reason; every control test must reproduce the defect it guards). Run `pnpm vitest run apps/web/src/lib/auth/auth-audit.test.ts apps/web/src/db` → PASS.
- [ ] **Step 0.4: Record** the chosen design and its evidence in the PR body: for each R, how it is met, the test names and results; which candidate parts were kept, changed or dropped and which Bob finding (Rounds 1–8) each part answers; the manifest delta; the facts relied on.
- [ ] **Step 0.5: Bob reviews the spike.** Commit (`feat(auth): auth_audit_log design spike (Task 31 Step 0)` with the trailer), push, open PR 4 as a **draft**, and ask Bob for a review round on that head (`review <n>`). Bob reviews the design and the tests as real code. Fixes are made in the spike (code and test) and re-reviewed. Tasks 12–15 and Task 31 Steps 1–4 start only after Bob's round on the spike says Approved or Approved with conditions. The PR's final review later covers the whole PR as usual.

**Exit criteria (all, before Step 0.5 asks Bob):** every R1–R15 has at least one named passing test; every T11 failure point passes; T6 and T7 pass in both orders and both directions; every control reproduces its defect; the facts are re-read; the manifest delta is within R14; `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint` exit 0.

**Stop rule.** If a requirement cannot be met without weakening it (for example R11 only with an operator reset, or R6 only by accepting a surviving password-only session), the builder **stops** Task 31 and PR 4, and brings it to Charlie in plain text: which requirement, what was tried and the evidence, and a **spec revision proposal** (the exact weaker wording, why, and what it costs). Nothing weaker is built until Charlie decides and the spec revision is reviewed and approved.

#### 31.D Steps after Bob's spike round (after Task 15)

- [ ] **Step 1: Apply the spike round's conditions,** if any, and keep every T test passing.
- [ ] **Step 2: Runbook and decisions.**
  - `docs/runbooks/phase-1-database-roles.md`: an `auth_audit_log` row in the grants table (what `crm_app` holds; no runtime reader; operators read it only through the admin connection, read-only, for an incident); a section describing the chosen mechanism, that only a migration may change it, that a 2FA change (enabling 2FA, disabling it, regenerating recovery codes) signs the user out everywhere else and that signing in with a recovery code does not (AUTH-07, R5), that the upgrade from a Phase 0 database signs every user out once (R15; announced in the upgrade notes before a production upgrade), what the user does after an interrupted enrolment (R11), that trusted devices are off, how a login maps to rows, and any incident query the design needs.
  - `docs/DECISIONS.md`: an entry "Phase 1 PR 4: `auth_audit_log` design (Task 31 spike)" recording the chosen design against R1–R15, the evidence, Bob's spike round, the open points as ruled (31.D open points A–G) and any residual.
- [ ] **Step 3: Run the PR 4 suite.** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test` → exit 0 (Docker running). `pnpm test:e2e` → PASS (if a Phase 0 test relies on a second session surviving a 2FA enable, a disable or a recovery-code regeneration, it now fails by design under AUTH-07: record it in the PR and change the test, not the mechanism).
- [ ] **Step 4: Commit and PR checkpoint.**
```bash
git add apps/web docs
```
