Mark PR 4 ready for review. Self-check rows: AUTH-01, AUTH-02, AUTH-03, AUTH-07, AUTH-12, TEN-02, TEN-03, TEN-06–TEN-11, UNI-03, UNI-09, UNI-10, §9 `crm_purge` (b1), and §9 no-RLS system tables (with `auth_audit_log`).

**Open points for Bob (requirement level; reviewed in this plan):**
- **A. R11 recovery policy (Bob Round 8).** Which outcome is acceptable after an interrupted or failed enrolment? Options, none picked, none verified:
  1. *Confirm-then-flip:* 2FA becomes "on" (and R5's revocation runs) only in the same statement that marks TOTP verified, so before it the account is still 2FA-off, sessions are untouched, and an interrupted enrolment is an unverified one the user simply restarts.
  2. *Revoke when TOTP becomes verified:* keep Better Auth's order (flag first) but end the other sessions only when `two_factor.verified` becomes true, so the changer's old session survives an interruption and can retry; the state "flag on, TOTP unverified" must then still not lock out a fresh sign-in.
  3. *Restartable enrolment at sign-in:* a user whose flag is on but who has no verified TOTP is sent to restart enrolment instead of a challenge it cannot complete. Bob rules whether this weakens 2FA (it must never apply to a completed enrolment).
  4. *Compensating undo* (turn the flag back off when a later step fails): listed for completeness only; the undo can itself fail, so it is not proposed.

  **Not acceptable as final without Charlie:** an operator reset (runbook), or any time-based automatic disable.
- **B. Is R1–R15 complete and correctly scoped?** In particular: the definition of "login" (R3), R6's mirror for disable, R11 applied to disable (builder addition), and R14's "at most `crm_app` INSERT".
- **C. The Better Auth password-step session (R2 residual).** 1.7.6 inserts a full session at the password step of a 2FA sign-in and deletes it in the same request; if that delete fails, the row outlives the request. Proposal: acceptable if it is audited (not a login), its token is in no response and reachable through no enabled endpoint (R10), and the runbook lists it; raise upstream.
- **D. Event vocabulary.** The candidate names (`session_issued` with reasons `sign_in` / `two_factor_pending` / `rotation`; `two_factor_verified`; `two_factor_enabled`; `two_factor_disabled`; `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`, because the database cannot tell a regeneration from a used code) and the not-recorded list (31.E). Bob confirms or trims; E12 states the categories.
- **E. Retention and erasure (PDPA).** Rows are kept in Phase 1; `user_id` is `on delete restrict` and no user deletion is enabled (line 747); `ip` stored raw as in `audit_log`. Whether rows are erased or redacted with the user, by which role, or kept for a fixed period, is decided with workspace offboarding and the retention statement (spec line 837; Phase 9) and recorded as an open item in the PR 4 DECISIONS entry. Charlie decides if the answer changes a spec rule.
- **F. Which recovery-code actions end other sessions (Bob plan review Round 9, P2; a security-policy choice).** Builder proposal (the definition at the top of 31.A, used by R5, T5 and E12): regeneration is a 2FA change and ends the user's other sessions; **consuming a code to sign in is not**: it is a login method, audited atomically as a login with `method` `backup_code` and as a recovery-code change event (R1, R3, R4), and it ends no other session. Why: a recovery code is the fallback for a lost or unavailable authenticator, typically used on a new device; revoking every other device each time one is used would sign the user out of the devices they still have, and gains little, because an attacker holding a code and the password can already sign in, and the user's response to a leaked code (regenerate, which does revoke) is unchanged. The cost: a session signed in with a stolen code does not by itself end the user's other sessions (it does not need to; it is a new session, audited as such). Bob rules whether this reading of AUTH-07 ("sessions revoked on … 2FA … change", spec line 748) is acceptable; if Bob or Charlie wants consumption to revoke too, R5, T5 (negative case becomes a positive case) and E12 (a), (d), (f) change together, and Charlie decides, since it is a security policy.
- **G. Phase 0 session cutover (Bob plan review Round 9, P1).** Builder proposal R15: invalidate every pre-existing session in the migration that installs the audit mechanism, before the mechanism goes live, with the `session` table locked, and fabricate no audit row; everyone signs in again once. Rejected for now: recording them as `session_issued` with reason `pre_existing` (new category, E12 change). In the builder's reading this changes no spec contract: it is how E12's existing "no session exists without its row" holds on an upgraded database, and E12 (a), (d), (f) state it so Charlie sees it in revision 8. It needs Charlie's own decision only if Bob rules that it changes the spec contract (or prefers the `pre_existing` alternative, which would add an event category).

#### 31.E Candidate designs (builder proposals from Rounds 1–7; NOT final; the spike may replace them)

Everything in this section is a **candidate**: written on paper against the 1.7.6 tarballs, reviewed by Bob in Rounds 1–8, **not reviewed to completion and not claimed sound**. It is kept so the spike starts from it and so Bob can see what each round answered. Step numbers inside this section ("Step 2", "Step 3", …) refer to the candidate steps below, not to 31.C/31.D. Words such as "fail-closed", "no window", "proves" and "is sound" in this section describe what the candidate is designed to do; they are claims for the spike to test, not settled results.

**Which Bob finding each candidate part answered:**

| Candidate part | Answered | Status |
|---|---|---|
| No application writer; every row by an `AFTER` row trigger in the same statement (`session_issued_audit`, `user_two_factor_audit`, `two_factor_backup_codes_audit`) | Rounds 4 and 5, P1 (after-hook writer: a double fault or process death left a usable session with no row) | candidate |
| `session.issue_reason` label set by `beforeSessionCreate`; `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` instead of `…_regenerated` | Round 3 (mislabelled recovery-code sign-ins) | candidate |
| `session_issuance_immutable` (token, user and label cannot change) and the `/list-sessions` token strip (`crmSessionListGuard`) | Rounds 4 and 5, P1 (another session could read a failed login's token) | candidate |
| One flag read (`beforeSessionCreate`) + `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` + `checkPasswordStepOutcome` | Round 6, P1 (reason and challenge from two different reads of the flag) | candidate |
| Trusted devices: migration 0018 refusal trigger and delete, `crmTwoFactorGuard` strip and expiry, `trustDevice: true` refused | Round 6, P1 (Phase 0 trust cookie skips the challenge) | candidate |
| `session_sign_in_two_factor_check` (`FOR SHARE` re-check) + `user_two_factor_revoke_sessions` (revocation in the flag's statement) | Round 7, P1 (password-only session survives a concurrent enable) | candidate; **causes the Round 8 lockout** |

**Unsolved: Bob plan review Round 8, P1 (2FA enrolment lockout).** With `user_two_factor_revoke_sessions`, the enable's flag `UPDATE` (`totp/index.mjs` line 208) deletes **every** session of the user, the changer's included, in its own statement. 1.7.6 then inserts the replacement session (line 210) and only later marks the TOTP row verified (`update({ verified: true })`, line 217), each a separate autocommit statement (fact iii, fact xiv). If the process dies, or the replacement insert or the `verified` write fails, after the flag statement committed, the user is left with 2FA on, an unverified TOTP row and no session: per Bob, the next sign-in demands a second step that the unverified enrolment cannot complete, and there is no session from which to restart enrolment, so only an operator reset helps. Stock 1.7.6 has the same flag-before-verified order but keeps the changer's old session until line 213, so an interruption there leaves a session to retry from; the candidate revocation removed that. **Likely directions (unverified; the spike decides with T11):** revoke when `two_factor.verified` becomes true instead of when the flag changes; or confirm-then-flip (the "2FA on" state takes effect only together with `verified = true`); or make a "flag on, TOTP unverified" account restart enrolment at sign-in. See open point A for the policy question.

**Every audit row is written by a database trigger, in the same statement as the change it records (changed after Bob plan review Rounds 4 and 5, P1).** Round 3 put the login rows in a Better Auth after-hook that deleted the new session when the audit write failed; Bob showed that a double fault or a process death left a usable session with no row, whose token the user's other sessions could read through `/list-sessions`. There is now no application writer of `auth_audit_log`: a login token exists only as a `session` row, and the row's audit row is written by an `AFTER INSERT` trigger on `session` inside the same `INSERT` statement. The application's only part is a label on the new session row saying why it was issued; the label changes what the audit row says, never whether it is written.

**Events (closed list: only what line 835 names, "logins" and "2FA", mapped to the Phase 1 endpoints of line 747), and which trigger writes each:**

| `event` | Spec words | Written when (Better Auth 1.7.6) | Written by | `detail` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session_issued` | "logins" | **Every** insert into `session`, whatever the path. In Phase 1 these are: the password step of `/sign-in/email`; the second step of a 2FA sign-in (`/two-factor/verify-totp` or `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` with no session before the request); and the session rotations Better Auth does when 2FA is confirmed (`verify-totp` with a session), when 2FA is disabled, and on `/change-password` with `revokeOtherSessions` (fact i). | trigger on `session`, same statement as the insert (Step 3) | `reason` and `method` or `cause` (table below), and `session_id` |
| `two_factor_verified` | "2FA" (verify) | The second step of a 2FA sign-in succeeds, which is exactly when the session it creates carries the label `two_factor_totp` or `two_factor_backup_code`. | the same trigger, same statement, written before that session's `session_issued` row | `{"method":"totp" or "backup_code","session_id":…}` |
| `two_factor_enabled` | "2FA" (enrol) | `user.two_factor_enabled` changes from false (or null) to true. In 1.7.6 only `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a session does this (`dist/plugins/two-factor/totp/index.mjs`, lines 206–208: `internalAdapter.updateUser(user.id, { twoFactorEnabled: true })`, the **first** write of that branch). `/two-factor/enable` alone stores only an unverified `two_factor` row, because `skipVerificationOnEnable` is off and the OTP method is not configured. | trigger on `user`, same statement (Step 3) | none |
| `two_factor_disabled` | "2FA" (disable) | `user.two_factor_enabled` changes from true to false (or null). In 1.7.6 only `/two-factor/disable` does this (`dist/plugins/two-factor/index.mjs`, line 217: `updateUser(user.id, { twoFactorEnabled: false })`, the **first** write, before the `two_factor` row is deleted). | trigger on `user`, same statement (Step 3) | none |
| `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` | "2FA" (recovery codes, line 747) | `two_factor.backup_codes` changes on a verified row: `/two-factor/generate-backup-codes` (regeneration, `backup-codes/index.mjs` line 292) **or** a recovery code consumed by `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` (line 216, a compare-and-set `incrementOne`). | trigger on `two_factor`, same statement (Step 3) | none |

**The `session_issued` reasons.** The trigger cannot see the HTTP path, so a Better Auth database hook (`labelSessionIssue`, Step 5) writes one label into the new column `session.issue_reason` of the row being inserted; the trigger turns the label into `detail`:

| `session.issue_reason` (label) | Set when | `detail` the trigger writes (plus `session_id`) | A "login" (line 835)? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `password` | `/sign-in/email`, and the label hook's one read of `user.two_factor_enabled` (the "flag read", below) is false. `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` then makes the two-factor after-hook see 2FA off, so the session is kept and returned. | `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"password"}` | **yes** |
| `password_two_factor` | `/sign-in/email`, and the flag read is true: the password step of a 2FA sign-in. `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` makes the two-factor after-hook see 2FA on, so it deletes this session in the same request and starts the challenge (fact ix). No trust cookie can stop it (trusted-device records are refused and deleted, and the cookie is stripped; fact xii), and `crmTwoFactorGuard` fails the request if the session survives anyway (Step 5). | `{"reason":"two_factor_pending"}` | no: the login completes at the second step |
| `two_factor_totp` / `two_factor_backup_code` | the second step of a 2FA sign-in | `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"totp"}` / `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"backup_code"}`, after the `two_factor_verified` row | **yes** |
| `rotation_two_factor_enabled` | `verify-totp` with a session (2FA confirmed); also `/two-factor/enable` if OTP or `skipVerificationOnEnable` were ever configured | `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_enabled"}` | no: the same signed-in user gets a new token |
| `rotation_two_factor_disabled` | `/two-factor/disable` | `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_disabled"}` | no |
| `rotation_password_changed` | `/change-password` with `revokeOtherSessions` | `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"password_changed"}` | no |
| null (no label) | any other insert: a path not in the label map, a script, a Better Auth upgrade | `{"reason":"unlabelled"}` | none expected; the Step 2 tests assert no Phase 1 path produces it |

**How the rows map to "logins".** One completed login is exactly one `session_issued` row with `detail.reason = 'sign_in'`, whether the user signed in with a password alone or with a password and then a TOTP or recovery code. The other `session_issued` rows record the other login tokens Better Auth issues: the password step's session and the rotations. Each of those is a usable token while it exists, so each must have its row for "no login token without its audit row" to hold. They are not a new kind of audited action: they are the same login credential, and the reason says why it was issued. The event set stays inside "logins and 2FA"; `sign_in` is now a reason, not an event name, and E12 lists `session_issued`. `session_id` is an ID (line 835: "entries reference IDs"); it is not a foreign key, because sessions are deleted on sign-out and expiry while audit rows stay.

**One read of the 2FA flag decides both the reason and the challenge (changed after Bob plan review Round 6, P1).** In 1.7.6, `/sign-in/email` loads the user (`api/routes/sign-in.mjs` line 317) before it checks the password and inserts the session (line 352), and the two-factor after-hook decides the challenge from that earlier object (`ctx.context.newSession.user.twoFactorEnabled`, `plugins/two-factor/index.mjs` lines 249–251). Round 5's trigger read the flag again inside the `INSERT`, so an enable or disable committed between the two reads made the reason disagree with the result. Neither read can be moved into the other's statement: the session-create database hook receives only the session data and the endpoint context, not the user object the endpoint loaded (`db/with-hooks.mjs`, `toRun(actualData, context)`; the user is a local variable of the endpoint until `setSessionCookie`), and Better Auth opens no transaction (fact iii), so no lock could span its reads. Phase 1 therefore picks **one** value and makes both sides use it:
- **The flag read.** `beforeSessionCreate` (the `session.create.before` database hook) reads `user.two_factor_enabled` once, for `/sign-in/email` only (`internalAdapter.findUserById`, after the password check and immediately before the `INSERT`). It writes the label from that value (`password` or `password_two_factor`) and remembers `{ token, label, twoFactorEnabled }` for this request (a `WeakMap` keyed by the request's `ctx.context`, fact xiii, so the record lives exactly as long as the request and holds nothing across requests). The trigger turns the label into the reason and reads no other table.
- **The challenge follows it.** `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` is the `hooks.after` option of `betterAuth`, which 1.7.6 runs **before** every plugin after-hook (`api/dispatch.mjs`, `getHooks`, lines 149–166: the user hook is pushed first, plugin hooks after it), so before the two-factor plugin's. On `/sign-in/email` with a `newSession`, it takes the remembered value and replaces `newSession` with the same session and a user whose `twoFactorEnabled` is that value (`ctx.context.setNewSession`, the call the two-factor plugin itself uses); the two-factor hook then reads that object (`const data = ctx.context.newSession`). If no value is remembered for the session, it throws a plain `Error` (fail closed: a 500 with no queued cookie, fact vi).
- **Outcome check.** `crmTwoFactorGuard`'s after-hook on `/sign-in/email` runs after the two-factor plugin's (it is listed after `twoFactor` in `plugins`). For a `password_two_factor` session it requires that `newSession` is now null (the challenge was issued); for a `password` session, that `newSession` still names it. On any mismatch it deletes the session if it still exists and throws a plain `Error` (500, no cookie). It is a tripwire: with the two hooks above and the trusted-device layers below, no mismatch is reachable, and the race and trust-layer tests prove it.

So for every password step, the label (hence the `session_issued` reason) and the response (challenge or completed login) come from the same flag read, after the password check. A flag change committed **before** that read decides this sign-in. A flag change committed **after** it can no longer leave a session behind: the session insert is refused if the flag changed before the insert, and the change deletes the session if the insert came first (next section). The flag read is the most recent read in the request, so the decision is never older than the one stock 1.7.6 makes. The `user` object in a completed sign-in's JSON body is given the same `twoFactorEnabled` value, so the body agrees too.

**No session obtained without the second factor survives a 2FA enable (changed after Bob plan review Round 7, P1).** Round 6 let a flag change committed between the flag read and the session `INSERT` apply "from the next sign-in". For a 2FA **enable** that meant B, holding only the password, could insert a password-only session after A turned 2FA on, and keep it: stock 1.7.6 revokes no other session on a 2FA change (fact xiv: `verify-totp` sets the flag at `totp/index.mjs` line 208, then inserts A's rotated session at line 210 and deletes only A's old one at line 213; `/two-factor/disable` does the same at `index.mjs` lines 217, 229 and 232), and the plan's AUTH-07 test only looked at sessions that existed when the change ran. Two database triggers now close it, with no new privilege and no application step that could fail in between:
- **A 2FA enable or disable revokes every session in its own statement (AUTH-07; the regeneration case of R5 is not covered by this candidate, see "Why `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`" below).** `user_two_factor_revoke_sessions` (`AFTER UPDATE OF two_factor_enabled ON "user"`, same `WHEN` as the audit trigger) runs `DELETE FROM public.session WHERE user_id = NEW.id` inside the flag's `UPDATE`. The flag change, its `two_factor_enabled` / `two_factor_disabled` row and the end of every session that existed when it committed are one statement: they commit together or not at all (a failed audit insert rolls the revocation back too, so the forced-failure cases still see "sessions unchanged"). There is no window between the flag commit and the revocation. The changer's own old session is deleted too; Better Auth then inserts the rotated session it was going to insert anyway (line 210 / 229, from the in-memory session object, not from the row), and its `deleteSession` of the old token finds no row and does nothing (`db/with-hooks.mjs` lines 116–140: `deleteWithHooks` reads the row first and skips the delete when there is none). Spec line 748 ("All sessions revoked on password, 2FA or email change") asks for exactly this; Task 24 Step 2 checks it end to end.
- **A sign-in session is inserted only under the flag value its label was decided on.** `session_sign_in_two_factor_check` (`BEFORE INSERT ON session`, only for the sign-in labels `password`, `password_two_factor`, `two_factor_totp` and `two_factor_backup_code`) reads `SELECT coalesce(two_factor_enabled, false) FROM "user" WHERE id = NEW.user_id FOR SHARE` and raises (`P0001`, fixed message, no data) unless the value equals the one the label implies: false for `password`, true for the other three. The insert fails, `createSession` throws, and the request is a 500 with no cookie and no token (fact vi); no session row and no audit row exist (the statement is rolled back). The user signs in again and gets the result the committed flag calls for. Rotation labels and unlabelled inserts are not checked (a rotation is issued to the user who just made the change).
- **Why both orders are covered.** The flag `UPDATE` holds a `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` row lock on the user row until it commits; `FOR SHARE` conflicts with it; the sign-in's insert is one autocommit statement, so its `FOR SHARE` lasts until that insert commits (fact iii). (1) **Insert first:** the sign-in holds `FOR SHARE`, so the enable's `UPDATE` waits until the session row has committed; then the `UPDATE` runs, and its revocation trigger (a new statement inside the trigger, which in READ COMMITTED takes a fresh snapshot) sees and deletes that session. B may already have its cookie, but the row it names is gone: `/get-session` returns null. (2) **Flag `UPDATE` first:** the `FOR SHARE` waits until the `UPDATE` commits, and in READ COMMITTED a locking read that waited returns the **updated** row version, so the trigger sees 2FA on, the label `password` disagrees, and the insert is refused; the revocation that already committed had nothing of B's to delete. (3) **Flag committed before the insert started** (seam 2 in the Step 2 race tests): the same refusal, without a wait. (4) **Flag committed before the flag read:** the label already agrees, the insert passes, and the two-factor hook issues the challenge (seam 1). The disable direction (1→0) is the mirror image: a `password_two_factor` (or second-step) session whose decision predates a committed disable is refused, and one committed before the disable is deleted by it, so no stale-label session is ever issued in either direction.
- **Lock waits, deadlock and latency.** The sign-in takes the user row first (in the `BEFORE` trigger, before its own session row exists), then inserts the session and its audit row; the flag change takes the user row, inserts its audit row and deletes session rows. The sign-in never holds a session row the change could wait on (the new row does not exist while it waits, and an uncommitted new row is invisible to the change's `DELETE`), so there is no lock cycle between them. If PostgreSQL ever reports a deadlock (`40P01`) or the wait times out, the statement fails: the sign-in fails with no session, or the 2FA change fails and is rolled back with its audit row and revocation; both are fail-closed and retryable. The check function sets `lock_timeout = '5s'` (pinned in `proconfig`), so a stuck flag change cannot hold sign-ins of that user for longer. Cost: one primary-key row lock per sign-in insert; it waits only while an `UPDATE` of the **same** user row is in flight (the flag change, or any other user-row update such as a name change: a few milliseconds each), and an `UPDATE` of that user waits for in-flight sign-in inserts of that user. `FOR SHARE` locks of concurrent sign-ins of the same user do not conflict with each other, nor with the foreign-key check's `FOR KEY SHARE`.
- **Privileges.** Both functions are `SECURITY INVOKER` with `search_path = ''`, schema-qualified names, `EXECUTE` revoked from PUBLIC and owned by `crm_migrator`, exactly like the others (Step 3 controls). Run as `crm_app`, `FOR SHARE` uses its existing SELECT and UPDATE on `"user"` and the revocation uses its existing SELECT and DELETE on `session` (both measured in Task 3: Better Auth updates the user and deletes sessions as `crm_app`), so the manifest does not change.

**Trusted-device cookies minted under Phase 0 (changed after Bob plan review Round 6, P1).** Phase 0 installs the two-factor plugin without refusing `trustDevice`, so a direct `/two-factor/verify-totp` or `/verify-backup-code` request with `trustDevice: true` may have minted a `trust_device` cookie (`verify-two-factor.mjs` lines 41–56: a signed `<hmac>!trust-device-<random>` value and a `verification` row with that identifier, value = the user id, 30-day expiry). At password sign-in the two-factor after-hook accepts a cookie whose HMAC matches and whose `verification` row exists, belongs to the user and has not expired, then rotates the row and the cookie and **keeps the session** (`index.mjs` lines 252–270). 1.7.6 has no option that turns this off (the plugin options are `trustDeviceMaxAge` and the others in `plugins/two-factor/types.d.mts`; `trustDeviceMaxAge` changes only new rows and the cookie's max age, so a Phase 0 row stays valid). Phase 1 makes such a cookie unusable with two independent layers, either of which alone suffices for an existing cookie, plus a third that stops new ones:
1. **No trusted-device record exists (database).** Migration 0018 first creates a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF identifier` trigger on `verification` that refuses any row whose `identifier` starts with `trust-device-` (`23514`), then deletes every such row. With no row, the after-hook's lookup fails and it expires the cookie and starts the challenge (line 274 onward). The trigger comes before the delete, so a row minted while the migration runs is either deleted or refused. The identifiers are stored as written because Phase 1 sets no `verification.storeIdentifier` and no `secondaryStorage` (`db/verification-token-storage.mjs`: `processIdentifier` returns the identifier unchanged without that option); a test pins both, so a later change to either fails CI before the trigger can be bypassed.
2. **No trust cookie reaches Better Auth (request).** `crmTwoFactorGuard`'s before-hook runs for every endpoint, on `/api/auth/*`, `postToAuth` and server-side `auth.api` calls alike. When the `cookie` header holds a cookie named `better-auth.trust_device` or `__Secure-better-auth.trust_device`, it returns `{ context: { headers } }` with that header rebuilt without it (1.7.6 `dispatch.mjs` lines 211–219 `set` each returned header on the endpoint context's request headers; that `ctx.getSignedCookie` in better-call 1.4.0 reads those headers is not in the unpacked tarballs and is fact xii, checked at task time and pinned by a test), and its after-hook expires that cookie in the response (`expireCookie`, `Max-Age=0`) whenever the before-hook removed one in the same request. If fact xii does not hold, the strip moves to the two HTTP entry points instead (`route.ts` and `postToAuth`, which both build the `Request` the handler reads), with the same tests; layers 1 and 3 do not depend on it.
3. **Never minted again.** `enforceInputLimits` refuses `trustDevice: true` on the two verify paths with 400 (Step 5).

If layers 1 and 2 both failed, the outcome check above would still fail the sign-in (a `password_two_factor` session that survived the two-factor hook), so no trusted-device login can complete with a `two_factor_pending` reason. A user with an old cookie simply gets the challenge; the Phase 0 form never sent `trustDevice: true`, so no form user loses anything.

**Why `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` and not `…_regenerated` (Round 3).** At the database, regeneration and consumption are the same statement shape: an `UPDATE two_factor SET backup_codes = <new ciphertext> WHERE id = …` on a verified row, with no other column changed (the codes are encrypted with the app secret, so the trigger cannot count them). A trigger therefore cannot tell them apart, and a trigger that wrote `regenerated` would mislabel every recovery-code sign-in. A recovery-code sign-in therefore writes three rows (`two_factor_backup_codes_changed` from the `two_factor` trigger, then `two_factor_verified` `{"method":"backup_code"}` and `session_issued` `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"backup_code"}` from the `session` trigger), and a regeneration writes one. **Gap against R5 (Bob plan review Round 9, P2):** because the trigger cannot tell the two apart, this candidate cannot end the other sessions on a regeneration (R5 requires it) without also ending them on every recovery-code sign-in (R5 forbids it). The spike must end them where the path is known, for example in the `/two-factor/generate-backup-codes` request, and still meet R5's "commit together or not at all" for the new codes and the revocation (T5 (c), (c2)); that design is not written here.

**Not recorded,** because line 835 does not name them (each is listed so Bob can rule): failed sign-ins and failed 2FA codes (the rate limiter counts them, Tasks 21–23, and this plan reads "logins" as issued login tokens); sign-out, session revoke and session expiry (session deletes); session refresh and `/organization/set-active` (updates of `expires_at` and `active_organization_id` on an existing row, which issue no token); password change itself (its rotation is recorded); `/two-factor/enable` before it is confirmed (an unverified `two_factor` row: the backup-codes trigger ignores unverified rows, and the flag does not change); a `verify-totp` call by a user whose 2FA is already on and who holds a session (no change and no session); the delete of the `two_factor` row by `/two-factor/disable` (the flag trigger already records that disable). Member changes stay `audit_log` events (Phase 3).

**Atomicity: why no committed 2FA change and no login token can exist without its row.** Better Auth 1.7.6 runs sign-in and the 2FA endpoints as separate autocommit statements with no shared transaction (fact iii), so an application hook cannot share a transaction with the change. Every audit row is therefore written by an `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW` trigger on the changed row itself. A row trigger runs inside the statement that changes the row; if its `INSERT INTO auth_audit_log` fails, the statement fails and PostgreSQL rolls it back. For each kind of change:
- **2FA state** (flag, recovery codes): each change is a single statement and the first write of its branch (fact iv), so a failed audit insert leaves 2FA as it was (and, for the flag, leaves every session in place: the revocation is in the same statement), and the endpoint makes no later write.
- **Login tokens.** A token exists only as a `session` row: Phase 1 uses database sessions with no secondary storage and no cookie cache (AUTH-12), and the cookie carries the row's token. A row gets a token only by `INSERT`: Better Auth never updates `token` or `user_id` (fact xi), and a second trigger refuses any `UPDATE` that changes `token`, `user_id` or `issue_reason` (Step 3), so no later path can put a new token on an audited row. The `session_issued` row is written inside the session's `INSERT` statement. So:
  - if the audit insert fails, the `INSERT` fails and is rolled back: `createSession` throws before `setSessionCookie`, the endpoint returns no token in its body or cookies, and the request fails (fact vi);
  - if the process dies or the connection drops during the statement, the transaction never commits and neither row exists;
  - if it dies after the commit, both rows exist: the token was never sent, and it is audited.

  There is no step between issuing and auditing left to fail, and no clean-up that can itself fail. The label hook runs before the `INSERT`: if it throws, `createSession` throws and the request fails with no session (fail-closed); if it gives no label, the row is still written, as `unlabelled`.

The triggers need no help from Better Auth, so they also cover any later code path, script or upgrade that inserts a session or changes the same 2FA columns.

**What remains (stated for Bob, open point 1).** The **password step of a 2FA sign-in** is a Better Auth design property that this plan does not change: 1.7.6 inserts a full session at the password step and its two-factor after-hook deletes it in the same request (fact ix). If that delete fails or the process dies before it, the session row stays until its `expires_at`, for a user who has not passed the second factor. With this plan that row is audited (`two_factor_pending`, written in its own `INSERT`), and its token is reachable through no enabled endpoint: the failing request's response carries no session cookie and no `token` (an error thrown in an after-hook is not an `APIError`, so `dispatch.mjs` rethrows it and the router answers 500 without the queued headers, fact vi), and `/list-sessions`, the one endpoint that returned other sessions' tokens, no longer returns any token (below). The runbook lists such rows (Step 6). Removing the row itself needs an upstream change (a 2FA challenge that does not insert a session first). The same holds for the two fail-closed paths added in Round 6 (no remembered flag value, or the outcome check's mismatch): each throws a plain `Error`, so the response carries no session cookie and no token, and if its delete also fails the row stays as a `two_factor_pending` (or, for a `password` label, `sign_in`) session that no response named. The runbook query covers the `two_factor_pending` case; the `sign_in` case needs a lost in-memory record within one request (a code defect, not a race or a fault), and the Step 2 cases show neither path is reached in Phase 1. Either row is an audited token that was never sent, not an unaudited one. The Round 7 refusal (a sign-in session whose label disagrees with the committed flag) leaves nothing behind: it fails the `INSERT` itself, so there is no session row and no audit row to clean up.

**`/list-sessions` returns tokens in 1.7.6, and Phase 1 strips them (defence in depth).** `listSessions` (`dist/api/routes/session.mjs`, line 347) maps each active row through `parseSessionOutput`, which removes only fields declared `returned: false` (`@better-auth/core/dist/utils/db.mjs`, `filterOutputFields`), and the core `session.token` field has no such flag (`@better-auth/core/dist/db/get-tables.mjs`, line 105); read 2026-10-21, re-checked in Step 1 and by the test. So any active session of a user can read the token of every other active session of that user. Phase 1 needs the list only for display and "sign out everywhere" (line 748), which uses `/revoke-sessions` and `/revoke-other-sessions` and needs no token, and Phase 1 builds no session-list page. The `crmSessionListGuard()` plugin (Step 5) therefore rebuilds every `/list-sessions` response from an allow-list of fields (`id`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `expiresAt`, `ipAddress`, `userAgent`): no `token`, and no field added later. It is an after-hook, so it covers `/api/auth/*`, `postToAuth` and server-side `auth.api.listSessions` calls alike; if it throws, the request fails and nothing is returned. `/revoke-session` stays enabled (line 747), but its `token` body can now name only a token the caller already holds.

**Files:**
- Create: `apps/web/drizzle/0015_auth_audit_log.sql` (generated: the table and `session.issue_reason`), `apps/web/drizzle/0016_grants_auth_audit_log.sql` (custom), `apps/web/drizzle/0017_auth_audit_triggers.sql` (custom), `apps/web/drizzle/0018_revoke_trusted_devices.sql` (custom), `apps/web/src/db/system/auth-audit.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/session-audit.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/auth-audit.test.ts`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/db/schema/system.ts` (`authAuditLog`), `apps/web/src/db/schema/auth.ts` (regenerated: `session.issue_reason`), `apps/web/src/db/schema/index.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/system/index.ts` (export the event and label lists and types), `apps/web/src/lib/auth/config.ts` (`session.additionalFields.issueReason`, `databaseHooks.session.create.before`, `hooks.after: alignPasswordStepTwoFactor`, the `crmTwoFactorGuard()` and `crmSessionListGuard()` plugins), `apps/web/src/lib/auth/input-limits.ts` (refuse `trustDevice: true`), `apps/web/src/db/privileges.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/privileges.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/test/catalog.ts` (`NO_RLS_ALLOW_LIST`, `AUTH_AUDIT_TRIGGERS`), `apps/web/src/test/postgres.ts` (`startDatabase({ stopBefore })` and `applyRemaining()`), `apps/web/src/db/rls.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/lint-rules.test.ts`, `docs/runbooks/phase-1-database-roles.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`

**Interfaces:**
```ts
// apps/web/src/db/system/auth-audit.ts (system module, TEN-10): lists and types only; no writer
export const AUTH_EVENTS = ["session_issued", "two_factor_verified", "two_factor_enabled", "two_factor_disabled", "two_factor_backup_codes_changed"] as const;
export type AuthEvent = (typeof AUTH_EVENTS)[number];
export const SESSION_ISSUE_LABELS = ["password", "password_two_factor", "two_factor_totp", "two_factor_backup_code", "rotation_two_factor_enabled", "rotation_two_factor_disabled", "rotation_password_changed"] as const;
export type SessionIssueLabel = (typeof SESSION_ISSUE_LABELS)[number];
// apps/web/src/lib/auth/session-audit.ts
export function labelSessionIssue(session: { userId: string; token: string }, ctx: GenericEndpointContext | null | undefined): Promise<SessionIssueLabel | null>;
export async function beforeSessionCreate(session: Record<string, unknown>, ctx: GenericEndpointContext | null | undefined): Promise<{ data: { issueReason: SessionIssueLabel | null } }>;
export const alignPasswordStepTwoFactor: AuthMiddleware; // betterAuth({ hooks: { after } }): runs before every plugin after-hook
export function crmTwoFactorGuard(): BetterAuthPlugin; // id "crm-two-factor-guard": trust-cookie strip (before) and expiry (after); password-step outcome check (after)
export function crmSessionListGuard(): BetterAuthPlugin; // id "crm-session-list-guard"
```
`labelSessionIssue` is a closed map from the endpoint path:
- `/sign-in/email` → the **flag read**: `await ctx.context.internalAdapter.findUserById(session.userId)`; no user → throw (fail closed, no session); `twoFactorEnabled === true` → `password_two_factor`, otherwise `password`. `{ token: session.token, label, twoFactorEnabled }` is remembered for `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` and `checkPasswordStepOutcome` in a `WeakMap` keyed by `ctx.context` (one object per request, fact xiii);
- `/two-factor/verify-totp` → `two_factor_totp` when `await getSessionFromCtx(ctx)` is null (the second step: the same cached call `verifyTwoFactor` uses to choose its branch, fact viii), otherwise `rotation_two_factor_enabled`;
- `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` → `two_factor_backup_code` when there is no session, otherwise null (1.7.6 inserts no session on that branch);
- `/two-factor/enable` → `rotation_two_factor_enabled`; `/two-factor/disable` → `rotation_two_factor_disabled`; `/change-password` → `rotation_password_changed`;
- any other path, or no endpoint context → null.

`beforeSessionCreate` **always** returns `{ data: { issueReason: <label or null> } }`, never `undefined`: `createSession(…, override)` copies the old session's fields into the new row (`dist/db/internal-adapter.mjs`, `...rest`), so without an explicit value a rotation on an unlisted path would inherit the old row's label. Nothing in application code writes `auth_audit_log`; the only writers are the triggers, run as `crm_app` (invoker, Step 3). The lists live in the system module so the lint rule (TEN-10) keeps the schema away from `src/lib`; `session-audit.ts` imports only types and constants from `@/db/system`.

- **Candidate step 1: Check the 1.7.6 facts** in the installed `node_modules/better-auth/dist` and `node_modules/@better-auth/core/dist` (read on 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21 from the published 1.7.6 tarballs, unpacked under `.agent/pack-ba` and `.agent/pack-ba-core`; re-read at task time and record each fact in the PR body):
  - (i) **Every session insert on a Phase 1 path** (`grep -rn "createSession(" dist`): `/sign-in/email` (`api/routes/sign-in.mjs` line 352); the second step of 2FA (`plugins/two-factor/verify-two-factor.mjs` line 31); rotation on 2FA confirmation (`plugins/two-factor/totp/index.mjs` line 210; `plugins/two-factor/index.mjs` lines 119 and 142 run only with the OTP method or `skipVerificationOnEnable`, both off); rotation on disable (`index.mjs` line 229); `/change-password` with `revokeOtherSessions` (`api/routes/update-user.mjs` line 176, after the password update and `deleteUserSessions`); `/sign-up/email` only with auto sign-in (`sign-up.mjs` line 255; Phase 0 sets `autoSignIn: false`); `/verify-email` and `/change-email` (`email-verification.mjs`, `update-user.mjs` line 457; disabled in Phase 1). The other callers are plugins Phase 1 does not install (magic link, anonymous, admin, username, phone number, SIWE, email OTP, device authorization) or OAuth (Part 3). The trigger covers every one of them; this list only decides the labels.
  - (ii) `createSession` builds the row and calls `createWithHooks(data, "session")` (`dist/db/with-hooks.mjs`): each `databaseHooks.session.create.before(data, ctx)` runs with the current endpoint context (`tryGetCurrentAuthEndpointContext`), its `data` is merged into the row, then **one** adapter `create`. A hook that throws makes `createSession` throw. The hook receives the session data (with `userId` and the new `token`) and the endpoint context, **not** the user object the endpoint loaded; `/sign-in/email` keeps that object in a local variable until `setSessionCookie` puts it on `ctx.context.newSession` (`cookies/index.mjs` line 179). With an `override` (rotations) the old session's fields are spread into the new row before the hook runs.
  - (iii) **No shared transaction from Better Auth.** Sign-in and the 2FA endpoints make several separate adapter writes and open no transaction. `runWithTransaction` (`@better-auth/core/dist/context/transaction.mjs`) only reaches calls that go through `getCurrentAdapter`, which `internalAdapter` does but the two-factor plugin's direct `ctx.context.adapter.create/update/delete/incrementOne` calls do not. Hence triggers for every row (Step 3).
  - (iv) **The 2FA change is one statement, and the first write of its branch.** Enable: in `verify-totp` with a session and `twoFactor.verified !== true`, `updateUser(…, { twoFactorEnabled: true })` comes before the session rotation and before `update({ verified: true })`. Disable: `updateUser(…, { twoFactorEnabled: false })` comes before the `two_factor` delete and the rotation. Regenerate: one `update({ backupCodes })`. Consume: one `incrementOne` with `set: { backupCodes }` and a compare on the old value. If a trigger's insert fails, the endpoint throws at that write and makes no later write.
  - (v) **The flag is what Better Auth trusts, read from an object loaded earlier.** The two-factor sign-in after-hook starts a challenge only when `ctx.context.newSession.user.twoFactorEnabled` is true (`index.mjs` lines 249–251), so the flag, not the presence of a `two_factor` row, is the "2FA on" state the triggers record and the label hook splits `password` / `password_two_factor` on. That object is the one `/sign-in/email` loaded with `findUserByEmail` (`sign-in.mjs` line 317) before the password check and the session insert (line 352), in separate statements (fact iii): hence the one flag read and `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` (Bob plan review Round 6, P1).
  - (vi) **A failed session insert is a failed request with no token.** The driver error from `createSession` is not an `APIError`: `dispatch.mjs` rethrows it from the endpoint (no after-hook runs) and the router's `onError` (`dist/api/index.mjs`, line 195) logs it and the router (better-call 1.4.0) answers 500. The same holds for a non-`APIError` thrown in an after-hook (`runAfterHooks` rethrows it). The status and body are pinned by the Step 2 tests.
  - (vii) **`/list-sessions` returns `token`** (see "`/list-sessions` returns tokens in 1.7.6"); the Step 2 test asserts it on the unguarded instance before Step 5 adds the guard.
  - (viii) `getSessionFromCtx` caches its result on `ctx.context.session`, `null` included (`dist/api/routes/session.mjs`, line 246), and `verifyTwoFactor` takes the second-step branch exactly when it returns `null` (`verify-two-factor.mjs`, line 14). So the label hook's call returns the same answer the endpoint used.
  - (ix) **Password step of a 2FA user.** The two-factor sign-in after-hook (`index.mjs` lines 243–289) keeps the session only when a valid trust-device cookie is presented (lines 252–270: HMAC of `<userId>!<identifier>` matches, `findVerificationValue(identifier)` finds a row whose `value` is the user id and whose `expiresAt` is in the future; it then deletes that row, creates a new `trust-device-…` row and sets a renewed cookie); otherwise it expires any trust cookie (line 274) and calls `deleteSessionCookie`, `deleteSession` and `setNewSession(null)`. A trust-device cookie is minted only by `verify-totp` / `verify-backup-code` with `trustDevice: true` (`verify-two-factor.mjs`, lines 41–56, after the session insert and its cookie). Phase 0 refuses neither (`input-limits.ts` checks only passwords and names), so cookies may exist; the Phase 0 form sends `trustDevice: false`. `/two-factor/disable` also deletes the row of a presented trust cookie (`index.mjs` lines 233–239).
  - (x) Every path in the events and label tables is in `Object.values(auth.api)` (the same check as Task 24 Step 1), and the Drizzle adapter's `update` sets only the fields it is given (so `UPDATE OF two_factor_enabled`, `UPDATE OF backup_codes` and `UPDATE OF token, user_id, issue_reason` fire only on those writes, and the `WHEN` clauses in Step 3 ignore no-op writes).
  - (xi) **No path changes a session's token or user.** The `updateSession` callers set `expiresAt`/`updatedAt` (session refresh, `session.mjs` line 199), `activeOrganizationId` (`plugins/organization/adapter.mjs` line 398) or input-enabled additional fields (`/update-session`, disabled in Phase 1; `issueReason` is `input: false`). None sets `token`, `userId` or `issueReason`, so the immutability trigger breaks no Phase 1 path.
  - (xii) **Trusted-device records and cookies.** Identifiers are `trust-device-` + 32 random characters (`verify-two-factor.mjs` line 49, `index.mjs` line 261), stored as written when `verification.storeIdentifier` is unset (`db/verification-token-storage.mjs`, `processIdentifier`; `db/internal-adapter.mjs` lines 701–720) and in the database when there is no `secondaryStorage` (Phase 1 sets neither; the Step 2 test pins it). The cookie name is `createAuthCookie("trust_device")`: `better-auth.trust_device`, with the `__Secure-` prefix when `useSecureCookies` holds (`cookies/index.mjs` lines 20–34). The plugin has no option that disables trusted devices (`plugins/two-factor/types.d.mts`: `trustDeviceMaxAge` only sets the age of new rows and cookies). A before-hook's returned `context.headers` are `set` on the endpoint context's request headers (`api/dispatch.mjs` lines 211–219). **To confirm in the installed tree** (better-call 1.4.0 is not in the unpacked tarballs): `ctx.getSignedCookie` in the endpoint and in after-hooks reads those same headers (the strip depends on it; if not, the strip moves to `route.ts` and `postToAuth`, as stated above).
  - (xiii) **Hook order and `newSession`.** `getHooks` (`api/dispatch.mjs` lines 137–170) pushes `options.hooks.before` / `options.hooks.after` first and then every plugin's hooks in `plugins` order; `runAfterHooks` runs them in that order on one context. So `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` (`hooks.after`) runs before the two-factor plugin's after-hook, and `crmTwoFactorGuard`'s after-hook (listed after `twoFactor`) runs after it. `setNewSession` assigns `ctx.context.newSession` (`context/create-context.mjs` line 191), and the two-factor hook reads it at its start (`index.mjs` line 249). `createSession` builds the token before the database hook runs (`internal-adapter.mjs` line 273), so the hook and the after-hook see the same `token`. `ctx.context` is one object per request: `dispatchAuthEndpoint` copies the auth context into a fresh `context` for each call (`api/dispatch.mjs` lines 194–204), and a before-hook's returned context is merged with `defuReplaceArrays`, which keeps that nested object by reference as long as the hook returns no `context` key (ours returns only `headers`; `defu` is not in the unpacked tarballs, so this is confirmed in the installed tree); so the endpoint, the database hook (`tryGetCurrentAuthEndpointContext()`) and every after-hook share it, which is also what the two-factor plugin relies on for `newSession`. The guard's per-request records are keyed by it, never by the outer `ctx`, which the merge replaces.
  - (xiv) **A 2FA change revokes no other session in stock 1.7.6, and the steps after the flag write never re-read the caller's session row** (read 2026-10-21; Bob plan review Round 7). Enable (`verify-totp` with a session, unverified row): `updateUser(user.id, { twoFactorEnabled: true })` (`totp/index.mjs` line 208), then `createSession(user.id, false, activeSession)` from the in-memory session (line 210), then `deleteSession(activeSession.token)` (line 213), then `update({ verified: true })` (line 217). Disable: `updateUser(…, { twoFactorEnabled: false })` (`index.mjs` line 217), the `two_factor` delete, `createSession(…, ctx.context.session.session)` (line 229), `deleteSession(…)` (line 232). No call ends the user's other sessions. `deleteSession` → `deleteWithHooks` (`db/with-hooks.mjs` lines 116–140) looks the row up first and skips the delete when it is gone, so the revocation trigger deleting the caller's old row first breaks neither endpoint. Each `createSession` is one adapter `create`, one autocommit statement (fact iii), so the check trigger's `FOR SHARE` lasts exactly that statement. The second step's session insert (`verify-two-factor.mjs` line 31) runs after `consumeVerificationValue` and after the endpoint's own `two_factor` lookup, also as one statement.
  If any fact differs, stop and take the difference to Bob before Step 3.
- **Candidate step 2: Write the failing tests** in `apps/web/src/lib/auth/auth-audit.test.ts`. Setup: Testcontainers `startDatabase()`; a real Better Auth instance from `createAuth` on the `crm_app` pool, as in `auth.test.ts`; TOTP codes computed from the enrolment secret, as in `auth.test.ts`. Rows and state (`user.two_factor_enabled`, the `two_factor` row, its `verified` and `backup_codes` values, `session` rows) are read through `adminUrl`, because `crm_app` has no SELECT on the audit table. "The session invariant" below is this query through `adminUrl`, expected to return no row: `SELECT s.id FROM session s WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM auth_audit_log a WHERE a.event = 'session_issued' AND a.detail->>'session_id' = s.id::text)`. The cases:
  - **User with no workspace:** sign up (0 sessions and 0 rows: auto sign-in is off), then sign in → exactly **1** row: `event = 'session_issued'`, `user_id` = the user, `detail = {"reason":"sign_in","method":"password","session_id":<the new session's id>}`, `ip` = that session row's `ip_address` (null when empty) and `created_at` inside the test window. The user has no `member` row.
  - **User in two workspaces:** a user who owns W1 and W2 signs in → exactly **1** new row. It is a user event, so it is not copied per workspace. A catalog check in the same case shows `auth_audit_log` has no `workspace_id` column.
  - **2FA enable:** a signed-in user calls `/two-factor/enable` → 0 new rows (unverified row only). Then `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a valid code → exactly 2 rows: `two_factor_enabled` and `session_issued` `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_enabled"}` whose `session_id` is the rotated session (the one the response's cookie names). **AUTH-07:** the user also held a second session C (signed in before the enable); after the enable the user's `session` rows are exactly {the rotated session}, C's `/get-session` returns null, and the revocation wrote no audit row.
  - **2FA sign-in:** that user signs in with the password → exactly 1 row, `session_issued` `{"reason":"two_factor_pending"}` (label `password_two_factor`), whose `session_id` names a session that no longer exists (the two-factor plugin deleted it). Then `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a valid code → exactly 2 rows with the same `created_at` (one statement): `two_factor_verified` `{"method":"totp"}` and `session_issued` `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"totp"}`, both with the new session's id. The same flow through `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` stores `{"method":"backup_code"}` in both, plus the `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` row (3 rows at the second step).
  - **2FA disable:** `/two-factor/disable` with the password → exactly 2 rows: `two_factor_disabled` and `session_issued` `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_disabled"}` (the `two_factor` delete adds none). **AUTH-07:** as for enable, a second session C is gone and only the rotated session remains.
  - **Recovery codes:** `/two-factor/generate-backup-codes` → exactly 1 `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` row.
  - **Password change:** `/change-password` with `revokeOtherSessions: true` → exactly 1 row, `session_issued` `{"reason":"rotation","cause":"password_changed"}`; the revoked sessions add none.
  - **No double rows:** a user update that does not touch the flag (`/update-user` with a new name), a `two_factor` update that does not change `backup_codes` (`verified` set to true at confirmation; the failure counter on a wrong code), re-running `/two-factor/enable` before confirmation, a session refresh (`/get-session` after the refresh age, which updates `expires_at`) and `/organization/set-active` (which updates `active_organization_id`) → 0 new rows each.
  - **Failures write nothing:** a wrong password, a wrong TOTP code, a wrong backup code, and a disable with a wrong password → 0 new rows each.
  - **Both entry paths:** the no-workspace sign-in also runs through `signInAction` (the `postToAuth` forwarder, Task 15) → exactly 1 row. The label hook and the trigger sit below both entry paths.
  - **Every Phase 1 path is labelled:** after all cases above, no row in the file has `detail.reason = 'unlabelled'`; a direct `INSERT INTO session` as `crm_app` with no label (the "unlabelled" fixture) writes one row with `{"reason":"unlabelled",…}` (the trigger does not depend on the label).
  - **Trusted devices refused:** `/two-factor/verify-totp` and `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` with `trustDevice: true` → 400 and no session, no row, no trust-device cookie; with `trustDevice: false` (the Phase 0 form) → normal.
  - **A trust cookie minted under Phase 0 does not skip the challenge (migration path; Bob plan review Round 6, P1).** On its own `startDatabase({ stopBefore: "0018" })` instance (the helper applies the migrations up to, not including, 0018), a **Phase 0 instance** (test-only: `createAuth` as Phase 0 configured it, i.e. `twoFactor()` and the Phase 0 `enforceInputLimits`, without `crmTwoFactorGuard`, `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` or the `trustDevice` refusal) enrols a user in 2FA and completes a 2FA sign-in with `trustDevice: true`. The test asserts the old behaviour first, so it is not testing a non-problem: the response sets a `trust_device` cookie, a `verification` row with `identifier LIKE 'trust-device-%'` and `value` = the user id exists, and a second password sign-in on that instance with the cookie returns a live session and no challenge. Then the helper applies the remaining migrations, and through `adminUrl` no `trust-device-%` row is left. Then, on the **configured** Phase 1 instance, `/sign-in/email` with the password and that cookie (the renewed one from the old instance's second sign-in, and in a second run the first one):
    - the response is the challenge (`twoFactorRedirect: true`), with no non-empty session-token cookie and no `token` in the body, and its `Set-Cookie` expires the `trust_device` cookie (`Max-Age=0` or an `Expires` in the past, empty value);
    - through `adminUrl`: the attempt wrote exactly one `session_issued` row with `{"reason":"two_factor_pending"}` and no `sign_in` row, its session no longer exists, and no `trust-device-%` row exists;
    - `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a valid code (no `trustDevice`) → exactly one `session_issued` row with `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"totp"}` (and its `two_factor_verified` row) for this login, and no trust cookie is set.
  - **Each trust layer alone** (the same cookie, each layer tested with the other two removed in a test-only instance or fixture):
    - database only: the Phase 0 instance (no strip, no outcome check) after migration 0018 → challenge, the two-factor hook itself expires the cookie (line 274), 1 `two_factor_pending` row;
    - strip only: through `adminUrl`, the fixture re-inserts the old `trust-device-…` row with the trigger disabled for that one statement (`ALTER TABLE verification DISABLE TRIGGER verification_no_trust_device` as the owner, re-enabled at once, on this test's own database); the configured instance → challenge and an expired cookie, and the re-inserted row is still there unused (the two-factor hook never saw the cookie);
    - outcome check only: the same re-inserted row, the `verification` trigger kept disabled for the whole request (otherwise the two-factor hook's renewal insert is itself refused and the request fails earlier, which is the database layer again), and a test-only instance without the strip → the two-factor hook accepts the cookie and keeps the session, `crmTwoFactorGuard` deletes it and the request fails with the 500 of fact (vi), with no non-empty session cookie, no `token` and no renewed trust cookie; the only `session_issued` row is `two_factor_pending`; the session no longer exists. The fixture then re-enables the trigger and deletes the `trust-device-…` rows.
  - **Trusted-device records are refused:** as `crm_app`, `INSERT INTO verification (identifier, value, expires_at, …) VALUES ('trust-device-x', …)` and `UPDATE verification SET identifier = 'trust-device-x' WHERE …` fail with `23514`; the 2FA challenge rows (`2fa-…`, `2fa-attempts-…`) are written and consumed normally (the "2FA sign-in" case passes). A config test asserts `auth.options.verification?.storeIdentifier` and `auth.options.secondaryStorage` are both undefined (fact xii), so identifiers are stored as written.
  - **Concurrent 2FA flag change during a sign-in (orchestrated; Bob plan review Rounds 6 and 7, P1).** Two seams, both test-only spies on the configured instance's `internalAdapter` (`(await auth.$context).internalAdapter`), active for one `/sign-in/email` request B: **seam 1** wraps `findUserByEmail` so that, after it returns the user, it awaits a flag change before returning (between Better Auth's user read and the flag read); **seam 2** wraps `findUserById` during B so that, after it returns, it awaits a flag change (between the flag read and the session `INSERT`). The flag change is a **real** request from the same user's other session A: **0→1** = A's `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a valid code for an unverified enrolment (sets the flag, revokes every session, rotates A); **1→0** = A's `/two-factor/disable` with the password. Before B starts, the user also holds a third session C. Four runs (seam 1 and seam 2, each direction), each on a fresh user. For each run the test reads, through `adminUrl`, the `session_issued` rows written by B (by user and the request's time window), the user's `session` rows, and the flag after the run, and asserts:
    - seam 1, 0→1: the flag read sees 2FA on, so the label is `password_two_factor`; B's response is the challenge; B's session no longer exists; B wrote exactly one row, `two_factor_pending`, and no `sign_in` row; completing B's second step with a valid TOTP code writes exactly one `sign_in` row (`method: totp`);
    - seam 1, 1→0: the flag read sees 2FA off; B's response is a completed login whose cookie names a session that exists; B wrote exactly one row, `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"password"}`, and the body's `user.twoFactorEnabled` is false (B signed in after the disable committed, with what the account now requires);
    - **seam 2, 0→1 (Round 7: no password-only session survives the enable):** the flag read saw 2FA off; A's enable then commits; B's insert is refused by `session_sign_in_two_factor_check`. B's response is the 500 of fact (vi): no `Set-Cookie` with a non-empty session-token value and no `token` in the body. Through `adminUrl`: no `session` row was created by B (the user's rows are exactly {A's rotated session}), and B wrote **no** `auth_audit_log` row (no `sign_in`, no `two_factor_pending`). A's enable succeeded: 200, the flag is on, exactly one `two_factor_enabled` row and one rotation row, A's old session and C are gone (AUTH-07), and C's `/get-session` returns null. A fresh sign-in by B then gets the challenge (one `two_factor_pending` row) and completes only with a TOTP code;
    - **seam 2, 1→0 (Round 7: fail closed in the mirror direction):** the flag read saw 2FA on (label `password_two_factor`); A's disable commits; B's insert is refused the same way: 500, no cookie, no token, no session row from B, no audit row from B. A's disable succeeded with its 2 rows, and C is gone (AUTH-07). A fresh sign-in by B then completes with one `{"reason":"sign_in","method":"password"}` row. This replaces Round 6's stock outcome (a challenge with an empty `twoFactorMethods` list that no second step could complete, because the disable had deleted the `two_factor` row): the user ends in the same place (no session from that attempt; sign in again), but with one rule for both directions, no stale-label session row to delete, and nothing for the runbook;
    - in all four: the session invariant holds; the number of B's `sign_in` rows equals 1 exactly when B's response was a completed login; and after A's request every session that existed before A's flag `UPDATE` is gone except A's rotated one (AUTH-07).
  - **Lock order between the sign-in insert and the flag `UPDATE` (orchestrated in the database; Bob plan review Round 7, P1).** On its own `startDatabase()` instance (its fixtures never meet the trigger catalog test), the test adds through `adminUrl` a fixture `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on `auth_audit_log` that waits on `pg_advisory_xact_lock(<k>)` for one chosen `event` (a `WHEN` clause), holds that lock on the admin connection, and uses `pg_stat_activity` (`wait_event_type = 'Lock'`, by backend pid) to know when a request is blocked. No spy is used, so these are real requests end to end. Four runs, each on a fresh user that also holds a session C:
    - **L1, 0→1, sign-in insert first:** the fixture holds B's `session_issued` insert (B's `BEFORE` check has already taken `FOR SHARE` on the user row and passed: flag off, label `password`); A's `verify-totp` starts and the test waits until A's backend is waiting on a row lock (its flag `UPDATE` is blocked by B's `FOR SHARE`); the lock is released. Expected: B's insert commits first (B's response is a completed login with one `sign_in` row); then A's `UPDATE` runs and its revocation deletes B's session in the same statement. After both: the user's `session` rows are exactly {A's rotated session}; `/get-session` with B's cookie returns null, and so does C's; the flag is on; one `two_factor_enabled` row.
    - **L2, 0→1, flag `UPDATE` first:** the fixture holds A's `two_factor_enabled` insert, so A's `UPDATE` holds the user row lock with the flag uncommitted; B's sign-in starts, its flag read sees 2FA off (the change is not committed), and the test waits until B's backend is waiting on a row lock (the check trigger's `FOR SHARE`); the lock is released. Expected: A's `UPDATE` commits (C revoked); B's `FOR SHARE` returns the updated row, the label `password` disagrees, B's insert is refused: the same assertions as seam 2, 0→1 (500, no cookie, no token, no session row and no audit row from B).
    - **L1, 1→0** (B labelled `password_two_factor` holds `FOR SHARE`; A's disable waits): B's session commits, the two-factor hook deletes it and issues the challenge, then A's disable revokes every session. B has no session; its second step then fails (`TOTP_NOT_ENABLED`: the `two_factor` row is gone) and writes no row; a fresh sign-in completes with a password.
    - **L2, 1→0** (A's disable holds the lock uncommitted; B's flag read sees 2FA on): B's insert is refused as in seam 2, 1→0.
    - In all four: the session invariant holds, no deadlock is reported, each blocked request finishes within the `lock_timeout` once the lock is released, and the fixture trigger is dropped at the end. A fifth run holds A's `UPDATE` beyond 5 s: B's insert fails with `55P03` (lock timeout), no session row and no audit row, and A's change then commits normally.
    A **control** run of seam 1 in both directions on a test-only instance with the Round 5 label hook (every `/sign-in/email` session labelled `password`), without `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` and the outcome check, and with the Round 5 trigger body (the in-statement flag read) installed as a fixture on that test's own database, shows the disagreement Bob described in Round 6 (a completed login with a `two_factor_pending` row, and a challenge with a `sign_in` row), so the fix is not tested against a non-problem. A **second control** (Round 7) runs seam 2, 0→1 on its own database with `session_sign_in_two_factor_check` and `user_two_factor_revoke_sessions` dropped as the owner (the Round 6 design): B's response is a completed password-only login, and `/get-session` with B's cookie returns a live session while the flag is on, which is the bypass Bob described; the same run on the configured database gives the seam 2, 0→1 outcome above.
  - **The flag read is fail-closed:** a test-only spy that makes `findUserById` return null during a sign-in → the request fails with no session row, no audit row and no token; a spy that deletes the request's record before `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` runs → 500 with no non-empty session cookie and no `token`, the session row stays with its `sign_in` or `two_factor_pending` row (the session invariant holds), and no response named it.
  - **`/list-sessions` gives no token:** before the guard (a second instance built without `crmSessionListGuard()`, test-only) a user with sessions A and B calls `/list-sessions` from A and the response holds B's `token` (fact vii, so the guard is not testing a non-problem); with the configured instance the same call returns both sessions' `id` and no element has a `token` key or any key outside the allow-list; the same through a server-side `auth.api.listSessions` call.
  - **Event list:** the event list in the CHECK constraint (`pg_get_constraintdef`) equals `AUTH_EVENTS`; the label map's values equal `SESSION_ISSUE_LABELS`, and each label appears in the trigger's `CASE` (read with `pg_get_functiondef`).
  - **PII (UNI-09):** for every row this file writes, `row_to_json(auth_audit_log)::text` contains none of: the user's email, name or password, any TOTP code or backup code used, the TOTP secret, any session token, or the user agent; every `detail` has only the keys `reason`, `method`, `cause` and `session_id` (or `method` and `session_id` for `two_factor_verified`; none for the 2FA change rows).
  - **The session invariant** holds after every case in this file (checked in `afterEach`).
  - **Forced audit-write failure: the 2FA change is rolled back.** The test makes every insert into `auth_audit_log` fail by revoking `INSERT ON auth_audit_log FROM crm_app` through `adminUrl` (the triggers run as the invoking role, Step 3), and grants it back after each case. For each case it snapshots the state through `adminUrl` before the call and compares after:
    - **enable:** a user with an unverified enrolment calls `/two-factor/verify-totp` with a valid code → the 500 pinned in fact (vi), not 200; `user.two_factor_enabled` is still false, the `two_factor` row is still `verified = false`, the user's `session` rows are unchanged (no rotation), and 0 audit rows. Then, with INSERT granted back, a password sign-in gets **no** 2FA challenge (2FA is off), and the same code confirms enrolment normally (the 2 rows of "2FA enable").
    - **disable:** a user with 2FA on calls `/two-factor/disable` with the right password → 500; `user.two_factor_enabled` is still true, the `two_factor` row is unchanged (same `secret` and `backup_codes` ciphertext), sessions unchanged, 0 audit rows. With INSERT granted back, a password sign-in **still** gets the 2FA challenge, and a retried disable succeeds with its 2 rows.
    - **recovery-code regeneration:** `/two-factor/generate-backup-codes` → 500 and no codes in the body; `two_factor.backup_codes` is byte-equal to the snapshot; 0 audit rows. With INSERT granted back, one of the **old** recovery codes still completes a sign-in.
    - **recovery-code sign-in:** at the second step, `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` with a valid code → 500; `backup_codes` byte-equal (the code is not consumed), no new `session` row, 0 audit rows.
  - **Forced audit-write failure: no session exists without its row, and no token leaves the server.** With INSERT revoked as above:
    - `/sign-in/email` for a user without 2FA → 500; the response carries no `Set-Cookie` with a non-empty session-token or session-data value and its body has no `token`; through `adminUrl` the user's `session` rows are exactly those before the request; `/get-session` with every cookie from the response → `null`; 0 audit rows; the session invariant holds;
    - **an existing session cannot obtain or use the failed login's token** (Bob's Round 4 and 5 scenario): the user already holds session A (signed in before the revoke); the failing sign-in B as above; then A calls `/list-sessions` → exactly one element, A's `id`, with no `token` key; through `adminUrl` the user's session rows are exactly {A}, so no B token exists to present, and the same holds after INSERT is granted back;
    - the password step of a 2FA user → 500, no session row, no `two_factor` challenge cookie with a value, 0 audit rows;
    - the second step of a 2FA sign-in (`/two-factor/verify-totp` with no session) → the same assertions as the first bullet; the challenge has been consumed, so after INSERT is granted back the old two-factor cookie is refused (`INVALID_TWO_FACTOR_COOKIE`) and a fresh sign-in with the second step succeeds;
    - `/change-password` with `revokeOtherSessions` → 500; the test pins what 1.7.6 leaves (expected: the new password is set and the user's sessions are deleted, with no new session), and asserts no new session row and the session invariant;
    - the same sign-in through `signInAction` (`postToAuth`) → `SIGN_IN_FAILED`, and the stubbed Next cookie store received no non-empty session-token cookie;
    - each failure logs Better Auth's error line only; the captured log holds no session token, email, IP or user agent.
  - **Interruption inside the statement (process or connection death).** On its own `startDatabase()` instance (so its fixture never meets the trigger catalog test), the test adds through `adminUrl` a fixture `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on `auth_audit_log` that waits on `pg_advisory_xact_lock(<k>)`, holds that lock on the admin connection, starts a sign-in, waits until `pg_stat_activity` shows the `crm_app` backend waiting on it (the session row is inserted, the statement and its transaction are open), then `pg_terminate_backend(<pid>)` and releases the lock. Expected: the sign-in fails; the user has no session row and no audit row; the session invariant holds. A second run releases the lock without terminating: one session and one row (the fixture does not change the result). The fixture trigger is dropped at the end.
  - **The password-step session survives a failed Better Auth delete** (the residual stated above): a test-only spy makes `internalAdapter.deleteSession` throw during the two-factor plugin's sign-in after-hook. Expected: the response is the 500 of fact (vi) with no non-empty session cookie and no `token` in the body; the session row remains **with** its `two_factor_pending` row (the session invariant holds); the user's other session A calls `/list-sessions` and sees the row's `id` but no `token`; no response body or `Set-Cookie` captured in the case contains that row's token (scanned); the runbook query of Step 6 lists the row.

The SQL below uses the column names the generator writes (`ip_address`, `user_id`, `issue_reason`); if they differ, use the generated names.
