  - Run `pnpm db:generate --custom --name auth_audit_triggers` and write (owner `crm_migrator`, who runs migrations):
    ```sql
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_two_factor_flag() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    BEGIN
      INSERT INTO public.auth_audit_log (user_id, event)
      VALUES (NEW.id, CASE WHEN coalesce(NEW.two_factor_enabled, false) THEN 'two_factor_enabled' ELSE 'two_factor_disabled' END);
      RETURN NULL;
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_backup_codes() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    BEGIN
      INSERT INTO public.auth_audit_log (user_id, event) VALUES (NEW.user_id, 'two_factor_backup_codes_changed');
      RETURN NULL;
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_session_issued() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    DECLARE
      v_ip text := nullif(NEW.ip_address, '');
      v_detail jsonb;
    BEGIN
      v_detail := CASE NEW.issue_reason
        WHEN 'password' THEN '{"reason":"sign_in","method":"password"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'password_two_factor' THEN '{"reason":"two_factor_pending"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'two_factor_totp' THEN '{"reason":"sign_in","method":"totp"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'two_factor_backup_code' THEN '{"reason":"sign_in","method":"backup_code"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'rotation_two_factor_enabled' THEN '{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_enabled"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'rotation_two_factor_disabled' THEN '{"reason":"rotation","cause":"two_factor_disabled"}'::jsonb
        WHEN 'rotation_password_changed' THEN '{"reason":"rotation","cause":"password_changed"}'::jsonb
        ELSE '{"reason":"unlabelled"}'::jsonb
      END || jsonb_build_object('session_id', NEW.id);
      IF NEW.issue_reason IN ('two_factor_totp', 'two_factor_backup_code') THEN
        INSERT INTO public.auth_audit_log (user_id, event, detail, ip)
        VALUES (NEW.user_id, 'two_factor_verified', jsonb_build_object('method', v_detail ->> 'method', 'session_id', NEW.id), v_ip);
      END IF;
      INSERT INTO public.auth_audit_log (user_id, event, detail, ip) VALUES (NEW.user_id, 'session_issued', v_detail, v_ip);
      RETURN NULL;
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_session_forbid_reissue() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    BEGIN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'session: token, user and issue reason are immutable' USING ERRCODE = '23514';
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_session_two_factor_check() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = '' SET lock_timeout = '5s'
    AS $$
    DECLARE
      v_enabled boolean;
    BEGIN
      -- Blocks while a flag UPDATE of this user is in flight, then reads the committed value (READ COMMITTED).
      SELECT coalesce(u.two_factor_enabled, false) INTO v_enabled
        FROM public."user" u WHERE u.id = NEW.user_id
        FOR SHARE OF u;
      IF v_enabled IS DISTINCT FROM (NEW.issue_reason <> 'password') THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'session: 2FA state changed during sign-in' USING ERRCODE = 'P0001';
      END IF;
      RETURN NEW;
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_revoke_sessions_on_two_factor_change() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    BEGIN
      DELETE FROM public.session WHERE user_id = NEW.id;
      RETURN NULL;
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_session_two_factor_check() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_revoke_sessions_on_two_factor_change() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_audit_two_factor_flag() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_audit_backup_codes() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_audit_session_issued() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_session_forbid_reissue() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER user_two_factor_audit AFTER UPDATE OF two_factor_enabled ON public."user"
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (coalesce(OLD.two_factor_enabled, false) IS DISTINCT FROM coalesce(NEW.two_factor_enabled, false))
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_two_factor_flag();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER user_two_factor_revoke_sessions AFTER UPDATE OF two_factor_enabled ON public."user"
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (coalesce(OLD.two_factor_enabled, false) IS DISTINCT FROM coalesce(NEW.two_factor_enabled, false))
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_revoke_sessions_on_two_factor_change();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER user_two_factor_audit_insert AFTER INSERT ON public."user"
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (coalesce(NEW.two_factor_enabled, false))
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_two_factor_flag();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER two_factor_backup_codes_audit AFTER UPDATE OF backup_codes ON public.two_factor
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (OLD.backup_codes IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.backup_codes
                         AND OLD.verified IS DISTINCT FROM false AND NEW.verified IS DISTINCT FROM false)
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_backup_codes();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER session_sign_in_two_factor_check BEFORE INSERT ON public.session
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (NEW.issue_reason IN ('password', 'password_two_factor', 'two_factor_totp', 'two_factor_backup_code'))
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_session_two_factor_check();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER session_issued_audit AFTER INSERT ON public.session
      FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_audit_session_issued();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER session_issuance_immutable BEFORE UPDATE OF token, user_id, issue_reason ON public.session
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (OLD.token IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.token OR OLD.user_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.user_id
                         OR OLD.issue_reason IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.issue_reason)
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_session_forbid_reissue();
    ```
    The insert trigger on `user` never fires in 1.7.6 (sign-up cannot set the flag: `input: false`); it exists so no path creates a user with 2FA on and no row. The `verified` guard skips re-enrolment before confirmation, which rewrites the unverified row's codes (fact iv). `session_issued_audit` has no `WHEN`: every inserted session gets its row, labelled or not. The `CASE` reads only the row's label, never `user.two_factor_enabled`: Round 5 read the flag here, in a different statement from Better Auth's own read, and a concurrent flag change could make the two disagree (Bob plan review Round 6, P1). The label hook's one flag read now decides the label, and `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` makes the two-factor after-hook decide the challenge from that same value ("One read of the 2FA flag", above). The **check** trigger (`session_sign_in_two_factor_check`, Round 7) does read the flag, but only to refuse: it never chooses a reason, so the audit row still follows the label, and a mismatch means no row at all. It is `BEFORE INSERT` so the lock is taken before the session row exists (no lock cycle, "Lock waits, deadlock and latency" above); a user that does not exist gives `NULL`, which also refuses (the foreign key would refuse it anyway). The **revocation** trigger (`user_two_factor_revoke_sessions`) fires after `user_two_factor_audit` (same timing and event, so PostgreSQL runs them in name order); both are in the flag's statement, so their order does not change the result. Neither writes an audit row for the deleted sessions (session deletes are not recorded, "Not recorded" above).
  - **Invoker, not definer (decision; Bob rules, open point 1).** `SECURITY INVOKER` means each trigger's insert runs as the role whose statement fired it: `crm_app` at runtime, which holds `INSERT` on `auth_audit_log` for exactly this purpose, so **no role gains a privilege beyond that one grant** and TEN-02 is unchanged (no membership, no EXECUTE, nothing else in the manifest). `SECURITY DEFINER` was considered and rejected: it would run the insert as the owner `crm_migrator`, which owns every table, so any flaw in a function body or its name resolution would act with owner rights; it would also stop a missing `crm_app` grant from failing the change, hiding a privilege drift that the manifest test should catch. Its one gain is noted in open point 1 (with no application writer left, a definer form would let `crm_app` lose INSERT, so it could not forge rows). Risks of the invoker form, and the controls:
    - **Name resolution hijack:** `SET search_path = ''` and schema-qualified names (`public.auth_audit_log`, `public."user"`), so a role that could create objects in another schema cannot redirect the insert. No runtime role holds `CREATE` on any schema (manifest).
    - **Running with higher rights:** when the owner or the admin changes the flag or inserts a session (a migration, an incident fix), the body runs with their rights; it does only fixed inserts (or, for the two refusal functions, only raises; for the check function, one locking read of one user row and a raise; for the revocation function, a delete of that user's sessions, which is what such a flag change must do anyway), so nothing more is reachable.
    - **Direct calls:** a trigger function cannot be called outside a trigger (`trigger functions can only be called as triggers`), and `EXECUTE` is revoked from PUBLIC (explicitly here and by the migration 0001 default), and granted to no role. PostgreSQL does not check `EXECUTE` when a trigger fires, so the revoke does not stop the trigger.
    - **Turning a trigger off:** `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER` and `DROP TRIGGER` need table ownership (`crm_migrator` only); `SET session_replication_role = replica` needs superuser; `crm_app` has no `TRIGGER` privilege on any table (manifest). All are tested as `crm_app` in Step 4.
    - **Forged rows:** `crm_app` can insert any event for any user through its INSERT grant (needed because the triggers run as `crm_app`); the triggers do not widen that, and no application code uses it. Reading, changing or deleting rows stays impossible for every runtime role (append-only, Step 4).
  - No `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and no policy on `auth_audit_log`: the table keeps `relrowsecurity = false` and is on the allow-list. No grant goes to `crm_worker`, `crm_purge`, `crm_link_resolver` or PUBLIC. No runtime role gets UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE or SELECT (append-only, as TEN-03 requires for `audit_log`; no reader in Phase 1).
  - Run `pnpm db:generate --custom --name revoke_trusted_devices` and write (0018; Bob plan review Round 6, P1: no trusted-device record minted under Phase 0 survives, and none can be stored again):
    ```sql
    CREATE FUNCTION public.auth_refuse_trust_device() RETURNS trigger
      LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = ''
    AS $$
    BEGIN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'verification: trusted-device records are disabled' USING ERRCODE = '23514';
    END
    $$;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.auth_refuse_trust_device() FROM PUBLIC;
    --> statement-breakpoint
    CREATE TRIGGER verification_no_trust_device BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF identifier ON public.verification
      FOR EACH ROW WHEN (NEW.identifier LIKE 'trust-device-%')
      EXECUTE FUNCTION public.auth_refuse_trust_device();
    --> statement-breakpoint
    DELETE FROM public.verification WHERE identifier LIKE 'trust-device-%';
    ```
    The trigger is created **before** the delete: `CREATE TRIGGER` waits for in-flight writes on `verification` (its `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` lock), every later insert of a trust row is refused, and the delete then removes every row committed before it, whether or not drizzle runs the file in one transaction. The pattern has no `_` or `%` inside the prefix, so `LIKE` matches the literal prefix. Every other `verification` row (2FA challenges, attempt counters) is untouched.

  - `crmSessionListGuard()` in `session-audit.ts` returns `{ id: "crm-session-list-guard", hooks: { after: [{ matcher: (ctx) => ctx.path === "/list-sessions", handler: createAuthMiddleware(async (ctx) => …) }] } }`. The handler returns at once when `isAPIError(ctx.context.returned)`; otherwise, if `ctx.context.returned` is an array, it returns `ctx.json(returned.map(pickAllowedSessionFields))` with the allow-list `id`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `expiresAt`, `ipAddress`, `userAgent`; anything else throws `APIError("INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR")` (no unchecked shape is returned). `config.ts`: `plugins: [twoFactor({ issuer: "CRM" }), organization({ … }), crmTwoFactorGuard(), crmSessionListGuard()]` (`crmTwoFactorGuard` must come after `twoFactor`, fact xiii; a config test asserts the order) and `hooks: { before: enforceInputLimits, after: alignPasswordStepTwoFactor }`.
  - **One flag read, one decision.** `beforeSessionCreate` labels as in Interfaces; for `/sign-in/email` it records `{ token, label, twoFactorEnabled }` for the request (`WeakMap` keyed by `ctx.context`). `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` = `createAuthMiddleware(async (ctx) => …)`: returns at once unless `ctx.path === "/sign-in/email"` and `ctx.context.newSession` is set; reads the request's record and requires `record.token === newSession.session.token`, throwing `new Error("sign-in: no 2FA flag read for this session")` if there is no record or the token differs; calls `ctx.context.setNewSession({ session: newSession.session, user: { ...newSession.user, twoFactorEnabled: value } })`; and, when the endpoint's returned body is an object with a `user`, returns `ctx.json({ ...returned, user: { ...returned.user, twoFactorEnabled: value } })` so the body agrees (the two-factor hook replaces the body with the challenge when `value` is true). It never reads the database.
  - **`crmTwoFactorGuard()`** in `session-audit.ts` returns `{ id: "crm-two-factor-guard", hooks: { before: [stripTrustDeviceCookie], after: [expireTrustDeviceCookie, checkPasswordStepOutcome] } }`:
    - `stripTrustDeviceCookie` (matcher: every path): parses `ctx.headers?.get("cookie")` with `parseCookies` from `better-auth/cookies`; if it holds `better-auth.trust_device` or `__Secure-better-auth.trust_device`, it records the request (a `WeakSet` keyed by the request's `ctx.context`, fact xiii) and returns `{ context: { headers: new Headers({ cookie: <the other cookies, re-serialised> }) } }`; otherwise it returns nothing.
    - `expireTrustDeviceCookie` (matcher: every path): when the request is recorded, `expireCookie(ctx, ctx.context.createAuthCookie("trust_device"))` (the name the instance uses, with its attributes).
    - `checkPasswordStepOutcome` (matcher: `/sign-in/email`): reads the same per-request record (the session's token and label; no record → no session was created, nothing to check); `password_two_factor` with a non-null `newSession`, or `password` with a null `newSession`, is a mismatch: it deletes the session by token through `ctx.context.internalAdapter.deleteSession` (if it still exists) and throws a plain `Error`, so the router answers 500 with no queued cookie (fact vi). No mismatch → nothing.
  - **Trusted devices off in Phase 1.** `enforceInputLimits` (the Phase 0 before-hook in `input-limits.ts`) refuses `trustDevice: true` on `/two-factor/verify-totp` and `/two-factor/verify-backup-code` with 400 (`APIError("BAD_REQUEST")`, code `TRUST_DEVICE_DISABLED`), before the endpoint runs. Reason: line 747 does not list trusted devices among the Phase 1 2FA features, the Phase 0 form already sends `false`, and without trust-device cookies the `password_two_factor` label always means "challenge pending" (fact ix). Together with migration 0018 and `crmTwoFactorGuard`, no trusted-device cookie, old or new, can complete a sign-in in Phase 1. Builder proposal; open point 3.
  - **Failure handling:** none in application code. A failed trigger insert fails its statement; Better Auth throws at that write (facts iv and vi) and the request fails with nothing changed and no token issued. The user can retry (after a failed second step, by signing in again: the challenge was consumed). The Round 6 fail-closed paths (no flag value remembered, outcome mismatch) also throw plain errors: 500, no cookie, nothing to retry around. The Round 7 refusal (`P0001` from `session_sign_in_two_factor_check`) reaches the user the same way: the sign-in fails with no session, and the form shows its generic sign-in error (`SIGN_IN_FAILED` through `postToAuth`); signing in again gives the result the committed flag calls for. The sign-in form is not changed for this rare race.
  - Run `pnpm vitest run apps/web/src/lib/auth/auth-audit.test.ts apps/web/src/db` → PASS. Then `pnpm vitest run apps/web/src/lib/auth` and `apps/web/src/app/form-actions.test.ts` → PASS (the Phase 0 auth tests are unchanged).
- **Candidate step 6: Runbook and decisions.**
  - `docs/runbooks/phase-1-database-roles.md`: add an `auth_audit_log` row to the grants table. `crm_app` has INSERT only, used by the triggers, and no runtime role reads the table in Phase 1. An operator reads it only through the admin connection, read-only, for an incident. Add an "Auth audit triggers" section: the eight triggers and seven invoker functions (including the `verification` trusted-device refusal of 0018, the sign-in 2FA check and the revocation of every session on a 2FA change), that only a migration may change them, that enabling or disabling 2FA signs the user out everywhere else (AUTH-07; the changer keeps a rotated session), what the log line `session: 2FA state changed during sign-in` means (a sign-in raced a 2FA change and was refused; no action needed), that trusted devices are off (old records deleted by 0018, new ones refused, the cookie stripped and expired), how a login maps to rows (`session_issued` with reason `sign_in`), and the incident query for password-step sessions that outlived their request (a `session` row whose `session_issued` row has reason `two_factor_pending` and is older than a minute): delete those sessions through the admin connection.
  - `docs/DECISIONS.md`: add an entry "Phase 1 PR 4: `auth_audit_log` events, triggers and labels". It records:
    - the events table, the reasons table and how rows map to "logins", the not-recorded list and the `two_factor_backup_codes_changed` naming;
    - the triggers for every row (same statement, rolled back together), the immutability trigger, and why invoker;
    - the `/list-sessions` token strip and the trusted-device policy (input refusal, migration 0018 delete and refusal trigger, cookie strip and expiry, outcome check);
    - the one flag read for the password step and `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor` (Bob plan review Round 6), with the hook-order fact it relies on;
    - the Round 7 serialisation (Bob plan review Round 7): the sign-in check trigger (`FOR SHARE`, refuse on a stale label), the revocation of every session in the flag's own statement (AUTH-07, which stock 1.7.6 does not do for 2FA changes, fact xiv), the lock-order argument, the lock timeout and the fail-closed behaviour;
    - the stated password-step residual, with Bob's ruling;
    - the erasure point, as Bob and Charlie rule it.
- **Candidate step 7: Run the PR 4 suite.** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test` → exit 0 (Docker running). `pnpm test:e2e` → PASS (the sign-in, 2FA and sign-out specs are unchanged; if a Phase 0 test relies on a second session surviving a 2FA enable or disable, it now fails by design under AUTH-07: record it in the PR and change the test, not the trigger).
- **Candidate step 8 (commit and PR checkpoint):** superseded by 31.D Step 4.

**Candidate open points (they apply only if the spike keeps the design they concern; the requirement-level open points are A–G in 31.D):**
1. **Triggers and the invoker form.**
   - *Is a trigger acceptable under TEN-02, and is invoker the right form?* Proposal: yes, invoker (Step 3): no role gains a privilege beyond `crm_app`'s INSERT, `search_path` is fixed, PUBLIC has no EXECUTE, only `crm_migrator` can change or disable the triggers, and the catalog tests pin all of it. With no application writer left, `SECURITY DEFINER` would let `crm_app` lose INSERT on the table (no forged rows), at the cost of an owner-rights function and the loss of the grant-drift signal (and the forced-failure tests would revoke something else). Bob picks.
   - *The Better Auth password-step session:* moved to requirement level, open point C.
2. *Retention and erasure:* moved to requirement level, open point E.
3. *Event list and session semantics:* moved to requirement level, open point D; its part (d), the trusted-device layers, stays a candidate question under point 5 (c).
4. **`ip`.** The `session` trigger copies the new session's `ip_address` (what Better Auth derived for the request; Task 22 makes it the edge-set IP) into `session_issued` and `two_factor_verified`. The 2FA change rows (`two_factor_enabled`, `two_factor_disabled`, `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`) keep `ip` null: their triggers see no request, and `crm_app` has no UPDATE to fill it later. A 2FA change with a rotation carries the IP on the rotation's row. Bob confirms that is acceptable.
5. **Round 6 design (trusted devices and the 2FA flag race).** (a) *Does the reason-and-challenge alignment hold?* Proposal (candidate, for the spike to prove with T7): yes. The label hook's one flag read (after the password check, immediately before the insert) decides both the `session_issued` reason and, through `alignPasswordStepTwoFactor`, the two-factor plugin's challenge decision; the audit trigger reads no flag (the Round 7 check trigger reads it only to refuse a stale label, point 6). It relies on 1.7.6 running `options.hooks.after` before plugin after-hooks and on the plugin reading `ctx.context.newSession` (fact xiii), both pinned by the orchestrated race tests and re-read on every upgrade. Alternatives considered and rejected: carrying Better Auth's own earlier value into the insert (the session-create hook does not receive the user object, fact ii); serialising flag changes against the whole sign-in with a lock (Better Auth's reads and writes are separate autocommit statements on a pool, fact iii, so no lock can span them without replacing its endpoints; Round 7 locks only around the session `INSERT`, which is one statement, and refuses instead of waiting for a decision); recording the outcome afterwards with a `session_reclassified` row (the audit table is append-only and a second event would leave the first row wrong for a while, and a crash between the two would leave it wrong for good, so it is weaker). (b) *Is a decision taken at the flag read acceptable?* Only together with point 6: Round 6 let a flag change committed after that read apply from the next sign-in, which for a 2FA enable left a password-only session usable after the enable (Bob plan review Round 7, P1). Now the read still decides the reason and the challenge, but a session whose read is stale at insert time is refused, and one inserted before the change is revoked by it. (c) *Trusted devices:* three layers (database, request, input) plus the outcome check; existing trusted devices are revoked by migration with no user-visible loss for the form. Bob confirms or asks for changes. No audit requirement is narrowed by this design.
6. **Round 7 design (2FA enable against a concurrent password-only sign-in; AUTH-07 at that seam).** *Bob plan review Round 8 (P1) found that this design's revocation in the flag statement causes the enrolment lockout described under "Unsolved" above; the claims below are the Round 7 proposal and are not established.* (a) *Does the serialisation hold in every order?* Proposal (Round 7, candidate): yes ("No session obtained without the second factor survives a 2FA enable", above). The flag `UPDATE` revokes every session of the user in its own statement, and a sign-in session's `BEFORE INSERT` check takes `FOR SHARE` on the user row and refuses when the committed flag disagrees with the label: insert-first → the change waits, then deletes the session; change-first or change-already-committed → the insert is refused; no window exists between the flag commit and the revocation. Tested with spies (seams 1 and 2) and with database lock-order fixtures and real requests (L1, L2), both directions, plus a control that reproduces the Round 6 bypass. (b) *AUTH-07 for 2FA changes is now done by a trigger, not by Better Auth.* Fact xiv: stock 1.7.6 rotates only the changer's session on enable and disable. Spec line 748 requires every session revoked; doing it in the flag's statement is the only form with no gap and no failure between the change and the revocation. User-visible effect: enabling or disabling 2FA signs the user out on every other device (the changer keeps a rotated session). This is what line 748 already says, so no spec change is proposed; Task 24 Step 2 checks it end to end. (c) *The refusal is a 500.* A sign-in that loses this race fails with a generic error and no session; the user signs in again. The race needs the same account's 2FA change to land within one sign-in request, so it is rare; a dedicated error code is not worth an extra Better Auth hook. (d) *Locks:* no lock cycle (the sign-in locks the user row before its session row exists); `lock_timeout = '5s'` bounds a sign-in's wait; a deadlock or timeout fails closed on either side. Every `UPDATE` of a user row (also a name change) now waits for that user's in-flight sign-in inserts and vice versa: milliseconds. (e) *Both functions stay invoker* with the same controls as the others, and add no privilege. Bob confirms, or asks for a different form (for example an application after-hook for revocation, which this plan rejects because a failure between the flag commit and the revocation would reopen the gap). No audit requirement is narrowed, and no in-flight sign-in is allowed to finish with a stale decision, so this needs no security exception from Charlie.

## PR 6 — `feat/p1-events` (Tasks 19–20)

**Guardrails:** EVT-01, EVT-02 (n-a: no relay), EVT-03, EVT-05, EVT-06, TEN-03, TEN-05–09, TEN-08, UNI-03, UNI-09, UNI-10, UNI-19, MON-03 (money in event payloads as decimal strings). Known risks R6, R7.

### Task 19: `domain_event`, transactional enqueue, dispatch, `notification`

**Files:** Create `packages/core/src/events/names.ts`, `packages/core/src/events/names.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/events/payload.ts`, `packages/core/src/events/payload.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/schema/events.ts`, `apps/web/drizzle/0012_domain_event_notification.sql`, `apps/web/drizzle/0013_rls_domain_event_notification.sql` (custom), `apps/web/src/jobs/enqueue.ts`, `apps/web/src/jobs/domain-event.ts`, `apps/web/src/jobs/enqueue.test.ts`; modify `packages/core/package.json` (export `./events`), `apps/web/src/jobs/handlers.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/privileges.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/isolation.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/privileges.test.ts`.

**Interfaces:**
- core: `EVENT_NAMES` = the spec's Part 1 event set (23 names, "Domain events and jobs"), `type EventName`.
- core (`payload.ts`), the JSON-safe event boundary. JSON has no bigint (`JSON.stringify(1n)` throws), and money is `bigint` minor units, so **money crosses the event boundary as a decimal string**:
  ```ts
  export type MinorUnits = string & { readonly __brand: "MinorUnits" };   // canonical base-10 integer, e.g. "9007199254740993"
  export function minorUnits(amount: bigint): MinorUnits;                  // amount.toString(); throws RangeError on a negative amount (spec Money rules: no negatives)
  export function parseMinorUnits(value: string): bigint;                  // accepts only /^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$/, else throws; BigInt(value)
  export type EventPayloadValue = string /* uuid */ | MinorUnits | number /* safe integer only, e.g. a count */;
  export type EventPayload = Readonly<Record<string, EventPayloadValue>>;  // IDs and amounts only; no bigint member
  ```
  Producers write `{ invoiceId, amount: minorUnits(total) }`; consumers read `parseMinorUnits(payload.amount)`. A `number` is never used for money.
- web: `emitEvent(tx: Tx, event: { name: EventName; version: number; payload: EventPayload }): Promise<string>` — **first** validates the payload with `eventPayloadSchema` (Zod, in `enqueue.ts`): each value is a uuid string, a canonical decimal string (`/^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$/`) or `z.number().int().safe()`; a raw `bigint` value (from an untyped or `as` caller) is refused before any SQL with `EventPayloadError("payload.<field> is a bigint; encode money with minorUnits()")`, and any other value with `EventPayloadError("payload.<field> is not an ID or amount")` (field name only, never the value, UNI-09). Then it inserts the row (workspace from the transaction setting), then `await (await getSendBoss()).send("domain_event", { workspaceId, eventId }, { db: fromDrizzle(tx, sql), id: eventId })`; returns `eventId`. `enqueueJob(tx, queue, data: JobData)` — same `fromDrizzle(tx, sql)` pattern, for non-event jobs (`email.send`).
- web (`enqueue.ts`), the **job-data check shared by every producer**: `type JobData = Readonly<Record<\`${string}Id\`, string>>` and `jobDataSchema` (Zod) = an object with at least `workspaceId`, every key matching `/^[a-z][A-Za-z]*Id$/` and every value `z.uuid()`. Both `enqueueJob` and the job `emitEvent` sends call `assertJobData(data)` before `send`; a non-ID key or value is refused before any SQL with `JobDataError("job.<key> is not an ID")` (key only, never the value, UNI-09). This is the only path to `send` in app code: `getSendBoss()` is called only in `enqueue.ts` (checked by the "every planned producer" test below).
- worker: the `domain_event` handler runs `withWorkspace(data.workspaceId, tx => load event by id)`; unknown payload fields are ignored (EVT-03); not found → throws (job retries, then dead letter). Core handlers registry is empty in Phase 1.

- [ ] **Step 0: Branch** `feat/p1-events`.
- [ ] **Step 1: Schema.** `domain_event`: `id uuid pk default gen_random_uuid()`, `workspace_id uuid not null references organization(id)`, `name text not null`, `version integer not null`, `payload jsonb not null`, `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`; index `(workspace_id, created_at)`. `notification`: `id uuid pk`, `workspace_id uuid not null references organization(id)`, `user_id uuid not null references "user"(id) on delete cascade`, `kind text not null`, `target_type text not null`, `target_id uuid not null`, `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `read_at timestamptz null`; index `(workspace_id, user_id, read_at)`. RLS template; `crm_app`: `domain_event` SELECT, INSERT; `notification` SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE(`read_at`). `notification` has no producer in Phase 1 (its first is Phase 7, owner notifications); it ships as table, policies, grants and isolation fixture only.
- [ ] **Step 2: Failing tests** (`enqueue.test.ts`, `crm_app` + a worker on `crm_worker`):
  - **rollback**: `withWorkspace(A, tx => { emitEvent(tx, …); throw })` → 0 `domain_event` rows and 0 jobs (`pgboss.job_common` read via `adminUrl`), repeated 50 times (EVT-01);
  - **commit**: one row and one job whose id equals the event id; the worker's `domain_event` handler receives it and loads the row under A;
  - **invisible before commit**: while the transaction is open, a second connection (`adminUrl`) sees no job and the worker fetches nothing;
  - **same id twice** → one job;
  - **payload guard**: `emitEvent` with a string field holding an email (`a@x`) is refused by `eventPayloadSchema`, so a payload carries IDs and amounts only; so are a non-canonical amount (`"007"`, `"1.5"`, `"-1"`) and an unsafe number (`2 ** 53`);
  - **raw bigint refused before the transaction writes**: inside `withWorkspace(A, tx => …)`, `emitEvent(tx, { …, payload: { amount: 1n } as unknown as EventPayload })` rejects with `EventPayloadError` naming `payload.amount` and "minorUnits()"; a spy on `tx.execute`/`tx.insert` shows no statement after the `set_config`, and afterwards there are 0 `domain_event` rows and 0 jobs; the same call in a `.ts` fixture without the cast is a type error (`// @ts-expect-error` line in `enqueue.test.ts`, checked by `pnpm typecheck`);
  - **large amount round-trips exactly**: `emitEvent` with `{ invoiceId, amount: minorUnits(9007199254740993n) }` (2^53 + 1, beyond `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`) commits; through `adminUrl`, `select jsonb_typeof(payload->'amount'), payload->>'amount' from domain_event` returns `string`, `9007199254740993`; the worker's `domain_event` handler loads the row under A and `parseMinorUnits(row.payload.amount) === 9007199254740993n`; no step goes through `Number`;
  - **atomic with the large amount**: the same emit followed by a throw in the same `withWorkspace` → 0 `domain_event` rows and 0 jobs; the committed case → exactly one row and one job with `job.id = event.id` (event and job never apart);
  - **job data IDs only** (`PROJECT.md` §9): `enqueueJob(tx, "email.send", { workspaceId, userId, kind: "test" } as unknown as JobData)` rejects with `JobDataError` naming `job.kind`, and so do a non-uuid value (`{ workspaceId, userId: "a@x" }`) and a missing `workspaceId`; `{ workspaceId, userId }` is accepted; each refusal leaves 0 jobs. **Every planned producer**: a table-driven case lists every `enqueueJob`/`emitEvent` call site in Phase 1 (`domain_event` through `emitEvent` → `{ workspaceId, eventId }`, which has no app call site in Phase 1 and is run directly; `email.send` through `enqueueJob` in `settings/email/actions.ts` → `{ workspaceId, userId }`, a row added by Task 27 Step 1 when that producer lands), runs each, and asserts through `adminUrl` that the stored `pgboss.job_common.data` has only keys ending in `Id` with uuid values; a second check scans the non-test files under `apps/web/src` for `enqueueJob(`, `emitEvent(` and `getSendBoss(` and fails if an `enqueueJob`/`emitEvent` call site is not in that table or `getSendBoss(` appears outside `enqueue.ts` (so a new producer must be added and checked);
  - **unknown fields**: a job data object with an extra field is processed (EVT-03);
  - **cross-workspace**: job data with workspace B and an event id from A → handler throws "event not found" (RLS hides it), job retries;
  - **crm_app cannot read payloads** (repeated from Task 16 next to the producer).

  - core `payload.test.ts`: `minorUnits(0n)` = `"0"`; `minorUnits(9007199254740993n)` = `"9007199254740993"`; `minorUnits(-1n)` throws; `parseMinorUnits` round-trips `2n ** 63n - 1n` exactly and refuses `""`, `"01"`, `"1e3"`, `" 1"`, `"-1"`.

### Task 21: Limits, the `rate_limit` table and the atomic consume

**Files:** Create `apps/web/src/db/system/rate-limit.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/system/rate-limit.test.ts`, `apps/web/drizzle/0014_rate_limit.sql`; modify `apps/web/src/db/schema/system.ts`, `packages/core/src/security/limits.ts`, `packages/core/src/security/limits.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/db/privileges.ts`, `apps/web/src/test/catalog.ts` (`NO_RLS_ALLOW_LIST` gains `rate_limit`).

- [ ] **Step 0: Branch** `feat/p1-rate-limits`.
- [ ] **Step 1: Limits.** Add, each with the comment-free spec citation in the test name ("spec Rate limits, Numbers row N"): `AUTH_DEFAULT_LIMIT = { windowSeconds: 60, max: 100 }`, `SIGN_IN_IP_LIMIT = { 60, 10 }`, `SIGN_IN_ACCOUNT_FAILURE_LIMIT = { 900, 5 }`, `SIGN_UP_IP_LIMIT = { 3600, 10 }`, `TWO_FACTOR_VERIFY_IP_LIMIT = { 60, 10 }`, `PASSWORD_CHECK_IP_LIMIT = { 900, 10 }`, `RATE_LIMIT_ROW_RETENTION_SECONDS = 86_400` ("removes counter rows whose window ended more than 24 hours ago"). The password-reset, link, OTP and Turnstile-off rows are **not** added now (UNI-02: "a value is added when the feature that uses it lands"). `limits.test.ts` asserts each value.
- [ ] **Step 2: Table.** `rate_limit` (system table, no RLS, spec: Better Auth's rate-limit table; here owned by the app because Better Auth uses `customStorage`, Settled item 5): `key text primary key`, `count integer not null check (count >= 0)`, `window_start timestamptz not null`, `window_seconds integer not null check (window_seconds > 0)`. Grants `crm_app` SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE (manifest). `pnpm db:generate --name rate_limit`.
- [ ] **Step 3: Failing tests** (`rate-limit.test.ts`, `crm_app`):
  - grants exactly `max` in one window and refuses the next (`allowed: false`, `retryAfterSeconds` > 0);
  - a new window starts after `window_seconds`, using the injectable `now` (tests pass a timestamp; production passes `null` → `clock_timestamp()`);
  - **concurrency** (two `postgres` pools = two processes' worth of connections, 50 parallel calls, limit 10, count starting at 9): exactly one grant; **window boundary**: 20 parallel calls at a `now` past the window end start the new window once (final `count` equals the number granted, `window_start` equal for all granted);
  - **persistence**: a counter written by pool 1 is seen by a new pool 2 (restart);
  - `giveBack(key, reservedWindowStart)` decrements only when `window_start` matches and `count > 0`; after the window rolled it changes nothing;
  - `cleanupExpired(now)` deletes only rows with `window_start + window_seconds + 24 h < now`.
- [ ] **Step 4: Implement** (system module only, TEN-10):
  ```ts
  // consume: one statement; no returned row = refused
  INSERT INTO rate_limit AS r (key, count, window_start, window_seconds)
  VALUES ($1, 1, coalesce($4::timestamptz, clock_timestamp()), $3)
  ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET
    count = CASE WHEN r.window_start + make_interval(secs => r.window_seconds) <= coalesce($4::timestamptz, clock_timestamp())
                 THEN 1 ELSE r.count + 1 END,
    window_start = CASE WHEN r.window_start + make_interval(secs => r.window_seconds) <= coalesce($4::timestamptz, clock_timestamp())
                 THEN coalesce($4::timestamptz, clock_timestamp()) ELSE r.window_start END,
    window_seconds = EXCLUDED.window_seconds
  WHERE r.window_start + make_interval(secs => r.window_seconds) <= coalesce($4::timestamptz, clock_timestamp())
     OR r.count < $2
  RETURNING count, window_start;
  ```
  On refusal, a plain read of `window_start + window_seconds` computes `retryAfterSeconds` (read only for the header; the decision was atomic). `giveBack`: `UPDATE rate_limit SET count = count - 1 WHERE key = $1 AND window_start = $2 AND count > 0`. Keys are prefixed: `ba:` (Better Auth), `acct:` (account HMAC). Exported: `consume`, `giveBack`, `cleanupExpired`.
- [ ] **Step 5: Run** `pnpm vitest run apps/web/src/db/system/rate-limit.test.ts packages/core/src/security` → PASS. Commit: `git commit -m "feat(auth): database rate-limit counters with one atomic consume statement" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"`

### Task 22: One client-IP derivation for both auth paths; `forward.ts` change

**Files:** Create `apps/web/src/lib/auth/client-ip.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/client-ip.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/handle.ts`; modify `apps/web/src/lib/env.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/env.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/forward.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/auth/forward.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/api/auth/[...all]/route.ts`, `.env.example`, `docker/compose.dev.yml`.

**Interfaces:**
- env: `TRUSTED_CLIENT_IP_HEADER: "cf-connecting-ip" | "x-forwarded-for" | "none"`, required when `NODE_ENV=production` (start-up fails without it), default `none` otherwise.
- `CLIENT_IP_HEADER = "x-crm-client-ip"` (internal); `PLACEHOLDER_CLIENT_IP = "192.0.2.1"` (TEST-NET-1, RFC 5737: a valid address that is never a real client).
- `deriveClientIp(headers: Headers, setting): { ip: string; fallback: boolean }` — reads only the configured header; accepts exactly one valid IPv4 or IPv6 address (`node:net` `isIP`); IPv6 is reduced to its /64 prefix; anything else (missing, empty, list, invalid, setting `none`) → placeholder with `fallback: true` and one warning line `{"level":"warn","msg":"client ip fallback"}` without the value (UNI-09).
- `prepareAuthRequest(request: Request): Request` — new request with `X-Forwarded-For`, `Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP`, `CF-Connecting-IP` and `x-crm-client-ip` removed, then `x-crm-client-ip` set to the derived IP.
- `handleAuthRequest(request: Request): Promise<Response>` — `prepareAuthRequest` → (Task 23: account keys) → (Task 24: enabled paths) → `getAuth().handler(prepared)` → (Task 23: uniform 429).
- Better Auth config: `advanced.ipAddress.ipAddressHeaders: ["x-crm-client-ip"]`.

- [ ] **Step 2: `forward.ts` change** (spec "Required Phase 1 change to `forward.ts`"): remove `"x-forwarded-for"` from `FORWARDED_HEADERS`; build the forwarded request from the incoming `headers()` (all of `FORWARDED_HEADERS` plus the configured trusted header), then pass it through `handleAuthRequest` instead of `getAuth().handler`. `forward.test.ts` asserts the request Better Auth receives has `x-crm-client-ip` = the edge IP and no `x-forwarded-for`.
- [ ] **Step 3: Route.** `route.ts` `GET` and `POST` become `handleAuthRequest(request)` (the `toNextJsHandler` wrapper is replaced by the direct handler call; check in 1.7.6 that `toNextJsHandler` adds nothing but method dispatch before removing it, else keep it and pass the prepared request).

The 2FA change rows (`two_factor_enabled`, `two_factor_disabled`, `two_factor_backup_codes_changed`) keep `ip` null (Task 31 candidate open point 4). Please try again later."}`, headers `Retry-After` and `X-Retry-After` (same value), `Content-Type: application/json`, `Cache-Control: no-store`. Forms keep showing the Phase 0 message "Too many attempts. Wait a moment and try again." (`messages.ts`, status 429 already mapped).

Config: `disabledPaths` = every endpoint path of the built instance minus this set (computed once in `createAuth` from the instance's endpoints, so a new upstream path is disabled by default). `handleAuthRequest` also answers 404 to any path not in the set before calling Better Auth (second layer, and it covers `auth.api` paths with no HTTP route).

- [ ] **Step 4: Tests** (`cleanup.test.ts`): after the job runs, only expired counter rows are gone; only expired idempotency rows are gone, in every workspace, and no other workspace's rows are touched by a single `withWorkspace`; the schedules are registered by `startWorker` (`boss.getSchedules()` lists both); a test walks `apps/web/src/app/**/route.ts` and asserts no route imports `src/jobs/cleanup` (no HTTP trigger).

### Task 25: Nonce CSP and security headers

**Prerequisite:** ASK 6 (HSTS values) answered.

**Files:** Create `apps/web/proxy.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/security-headers.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/security-headers.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/security-headers.spec.ts`; modify `apps/web/next.config.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx`, `docs/runbooks/phase-1-security.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

**Interfaces:** `buildCsp(nonce: string, isDev: boolean): string`; `STATIC_SECURITY_HEADERS: { key: string; value: string }[]`.

- [ ] **Step 0: Branch** `feat/p1-headers-email`.
- [ ] **Step 1: Failing unit test.** `buildCsp` returns, in this order, `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-<n>' 'strict-dynamic'; style-src 'self' 'nonce-<n>'; img-src 'self' blob: data:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests` (Next 16 docs pattern); in dev it adds `'unsafe-eval'` to `script-src` and uses `'unsafe-inline'` for `style-src` (docs: React dev tooling); no `'unsafe-inline'` in production. `STATIC_SECURITY_HEADERS` = `Strict-Transport-Security` (value from ASK 6), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`, and for non-HTML responses `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`.
- [ ] **Step 2: Implement.** `proxy.ts` (Next 16 `proxy` export): nonce = base64 of 16 bytes from `crypto.getRandomValues`; sets `x-nonce` and `Content-Security-Policy` on the request and the response; matcher excludes `_next/static`, `_next/image`, `favicon.ico` and prefetches (docs). It reads no database (AUTH-11). `next.config.ts` `headers()` applies `STATIC_SECURITY_HEADERS` to `/(.*)` and the `default-src 'none'` CSP to `/api/(.*)`. The root layout calls `await connection()` so every page renders dynamically (docs: nonces need dynamic rendering; Risk 9). Cookies: Phase 0 already sets `Secure` for non-loopback origins, `HttpOnly` and `SameSite=Lax` through Better Auth; the E2E asserts them (UNI-18).
- [ ] **Step 3: E2E** (`security-headers.spec.ts`; runs in the existing `chromium`, `tablet-768` and `mobile-320` projects): on `/sign-in` and an authenticated page, the response has the CSP with a nonce that differs between two loads, every `<script>` carries that nonce, and the page records **zero** `securitypolicyviolation` events and zero console CSP errors through sign-in, 2FA enrolment and workspace switching; `/api/health` has `default-src 'none'`; the session cookie has `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and (behind `https` in the image job) `Secure`.
- [ ] **Step 4: Decisions (UNI-17):** where each header is applied (proxy for page CSP; `next.config.ts` for static headers; Cloudflare/Caddy add nothing that conflicts), and the HSTS values from ASK 6.
- [ ] **Step 5: Run** `pnpm vitest run apps/web/src/lib/security-headers.test.ts && pnpm test:e2e` → PASS. Commit: `git commit -m "feat(web): nonce CSP via proxy and static security headers" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"`

The idempotency key is a hidden `<input type="hidden" name="idempotencyKey">` generated per render (not a user control). It works without JavaScript (plain form post to the server action, server-rendered result). `send-test-email-form.tsx` is the only client component, and only for `useActionState` pending state (button `disabled` + "Sending…" while pending), the smallest component that owns that state (FE-31).

Error texts map the `action()` results: `FORBIDDEN` → "Only an Admin or Owner can send a test email."; `UNAUTHENTICATED` → redirect to `/sign-in` (as Phase 0); a network or server failure → the form is wrapped in the existing `SubmitFailureBoundary` and shows its own retry message (new constant in `messages.ts`, e.g. "We couldn't send the test email. The `expectAccessible` overflow check covers it at 320, 768 and desktop (Step 4).

## ASK list (for Charlie; nothing below is invented in code)

1. **ANSWERED 2026-10-20 (Charlie: "follow bob's suggestions"; own login; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-20).** *Original:* **Open question 8** — confirm `crm_purge` connects with **its own login** (Settled item 1), alternative `SET LOCAL ROLE` from a `crm_app` connection. Needed because `PROJECT.md` §11 makes every open-question choice Charlie's. Blocks: E3 only (Phase 1 code works either way until Phase 9, but Task 13's behavioural test is written for own login).
2. **ANSWERED 2026-10-20 (Charlie: "follow bob's suggestions"; yes, move; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-20).** *Original:* **Move the polymorphic-reference and `version_retention` tests** from the Phase 1 done-when to the phase that creates each table, with Phase 1 testing the generic helper on fixtures (Settled item 3). Blocks: E1, E2, E4, E5, and Phase 1's done-when sign-off.
3. **ANSWERED 2026-10-20 (Charlie: "B"; a new no-RLS `auth_audit_log` table; `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-10-20, ASK 3 = B).** The builder asked in plain text: "Option A: one audit row per workspace the user belongs to; the spec gets an exception for a user with no workspace" or "Option B: a new `auth_audit_log` table outside the workspace rules; every login recorded; a bigger spec change" (builder pick: B). Charlie answered: "B". Effect: spec edit E12 in Task 30 (revision 8, reviewed by Bob there); Task 31 builds the table in PR 4. *Original:* **Audit of user-level auth events (Bob plan Rounds 1 and 2, P1).** The spec ("Audit, logging, privacy") says the audit log "records logins, 2FA, … member changes", and `audit_log` is a tenant table with a non-null `workspace_id` under RLS; a sign-in or a 2FA change belongs to a user, not a workspace. Options were:
   - **(a) One audit row per workspace the user belongs to at that moment** (no spec change; a user with **no** workspace leaves no record, and a workspace joined later does not see earlier events) — rejected.
   - **(b) A new no-RLS system table `auth_audit_log`** (user-keyed, append-only, reached only through the system module; records every event, including for a user with no workspace; changes the spec) — **chosen**.
   - **(c) Defer auth-event auditing to a named later phase** (moves a Phase 1 done-when clause by a spec revision) — rejected.
4. **Idempotency key expiry** (`IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_EXPIRY_SECONDS`): not in the spec. Builder proposal: 24 hours. Blocks Task 14 Step 4 onwards.
5. **`billing_account.status` values.** Not in the spec. Builder proposal for Part 1: only `active` (CHECK), more states with plans in Part 3.
6. **HSTS:** `max-age` (proposal 31536000), `includeSubDomains` (proposal: no, because it would also bind other subdomains of the domain to HTTPS), `preload` (proposal: no). Blocks Task 25.
7. **Production `EMAIL_FROM`** address and display name for the SaaS (and the sending domain's SPF/DKIM/DMARC owner). Dev uses `CRM Dev <dev@localhost>`. Blocks only the production runbook line.
8. **Test-email wording** (subject and body; draft in Task 26 Step 4) **and the `/settings/email` page copy** (intro line, button, "Test email queued.", the `FORBIDDEN` and failure messages; drafts in Task 27 Step 3). Blocks Task 27's commit.
9. **`require_2fa` enforcement**: the Phase 0 plan listed it for Phase 1, the spec's Phase 1 row does not. Builder proposal: Phase 2 (with the settings page that sets it). Confirm.

## Risks

1. **pg-boss maintenance needs ownership for some passes.** `reindex` (default on) runs `REINDEX INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, and `persistQueueStats` creates/drops partitions; `crm_worker` owns nothing. Mitigation: both off in the worker; index bloat checks become a `crm_migrator` runbook task. If a 12.35.0 maintenance pass still needs DDL, Task 17 finds it (`42501`) and the plan comes back to Bob before any extra grant.
2. **Better Auth upgrade drift.** Paths, hooks and limiter internals were read from 1.7.6. The enabled-path snapshot test and the customStorage choice fail loudly on change; an upgrade is a reviewed PR.
3. **Spec says Better Auth skips its limiter without an IP; 1.7.6 uses a shared `no-trusted-ip` key.** Harmless here (we always set an IP), but the text is wrong (E6).
4. **`job_now()` is not executable through PUBLIC any more**, contrary to the spec's grant comment (line 499); without the explicit grant the web `send` fails at runtime. Task 16 tests `send` as `crm_app`; E5b fixes the comment.
5. **Dev data loss.** Splitting the compose superuser from `crm` to role users needs `docker compose … down -v`; Phase 0 dev accounts are lost (runbook says so).
6. **Two writes on workspace creation** (Better Auth's organization row, then `workspace_settings` and `billing_account`) are not one transaction. Mitigation: `ensureWorkspaceSettings` on first use (Task 7) and the before-create hook for `billing_account` (Task 9); tests cover the heal path.
7. **CI time.** The gate test (75 s+), two `next start` processes and extra Testcontainers raise `checks` beyond 15 minutes. Timeout raised to 30; if it grows further, split a `gates` job.
8. **Rate limits on in tests and E2E** (`enabled: true`) can make the existing E2E suite hit 429 from one IP. The suite already paces with `E2E_RATE_LIMITED`; the IP limits are per 60 s and generous (100 default, 10 sign-in); the account key gives back on success. If flakes appear, E2E uses distinct test edge IPs per worker, never a disabled limiter.
9. **Nonce CSP makes every page dynamic** (Next docs). Acceptable for an authenticated app; client link pages (Phase 7) are dynamic anyway. Tailwind v4 output is a stylesheet, not inline, so `style-src` with a nonce holds; the E2E fails on any violation.
10. **Payload rule edge — resolved (Bob plan review Round 2).** No job carries a non-ID field: `kind` was removed from the `email.send` data, the handler derives the operation from the queue name (Task 27 Steps 1–2), and `assertJobData` refuses any non-ID key for every producer (Task 19). `workspaceId` stays in job data as an ID (Settled item 5).
11. **New packages younger than pnpm's release-age gate** (nodemailer 10.1.7 was two days old on 2026-10-20). The task takes the newest version the gate allows and records it.
12. **Spec text says `share_link_resolve` and `crm_link_resolver`'s grant**; Phase 1 creates the role with nothing. The RESTRICTIVE catalog test's `share_link_resolve` exception is coded as "allowed when present" and becomes mandatory in Phase 7.
13. **Better Auth 1.7.6 gives `auth_audit_log` no shared transaction** (sign-in and the 2FA endpoints run as separate autocommit writes; `runWithTransaction` does not reach the two-factor plugin's direct adapter calls; Task 31 candidate fact iii). Eight plan review rounds each found a new timing issue in these multi-statement flows. Mitigation (owner scope choice B, 2026-10-21): the plan fixes the requirements (Task 31 R1–R15) and the tests that prove them (T1–T15: forced audit failure at each step, interruption, orchestrated races in both orders and both directions, enrolment failure injection); the mechanism is built and proven against the installed version in the PR 4 design spike and reviewed by Bob as code before the rest of PR 4. The candidate design (31.E: a row trigger in the same statement as each change, one flag read for the password step, a database re-check and revocation) is the starting point, not a settled answer; Round 8 showed one part of it causes an enrolment lockout. Residual to rule (open point C): Better Auth's own password-step session for a 2FA user can outlive its request if the two-factor plugin's delete fails. Upgrade risk: a Better Auth upgrade that changes these flows is caught by the T tests and by re-reading the facts the chosen design relies on (Step 0.1).
14. **`auth_audit_log` grows without a purge** (one row per issued session and per 2FA event; no archive job in Part 1, as for `audit_log`). Small at 5–15 person agencies; the `(user_id, created_at)` index keeps reads fast. Erasure and retention for PDPA are open point E of Task 31 (31.D), not decided here.
15. **Trusted-device cookies minted under Phase 0** (Phase 0 did not refuse `trustDevice: true`; 1.7.6 accepts and renews a valid cookie at sign-in and has no option to turn trusted devices off). Candidate mitigation (Task 31 31.E, Bob plan review Round 6; R8 and R9 are binding, the layers are decided in the spike): migration 0018 creates a trigger that refuses any `trust-device-…` `verification` row and then deletes the existing ones; `crmTwoFactorGuard` strips the cookie from every request and expires it; `trustDevice: true` is refused; an outcome check fails any password step whose challenge did not happen. Each layer alone is tested. Residuals: the database layer depends on identifiers being stored as written (no `verification.storeIdentifier`, no `secondaryStorage`; a config test fails if either is set); the request strip depends on better-call reading the hook-replaced headers (fact xii, confirmed at task time, else the strip moves to the two HTTP entry points); a user with an old cookie loses nothing the form offered.
16. **Candidate design only: the sign-in 2FA check takes a row lock on the user (Task 31 31.E, Bob plan review Round 7).** This risk applies only if the spike keeps that check. Every sign-in session insert takes `FOR SHARE` on its user row, so it waits while an `UPDATE` of the same user row is in flight (a 2FA change, a name change) and such an `UPDATE` waits for that user's in-flight sign-in inserts. Waits are milliseconds and per user; there is no lock cycle (the sign-in locks the user row before its session row exists); `lock_timeout = '5s'` on the check function bounds a sign-in's wait. A timeout, a deadlock report or a refused stale label fails closed: the sign-in returns a generic 500 with no session and the user signs in again; a failed 2FA change is rolled back with its audit row and revocation. Residual: a user who signs in at the instant another of their sessions changes 2FA sees one failed sign-in. Upgrade risk: a Better Auth version that inserts sessions in a longer transaction (holding `FOR SHARE` longer) or that writes the flag with a different statement is caught by fact xiv and the lock-order tests.
17. **The Task 31 spike may show that a requirement cannot be met as written** (for example R11, no enrolment lockout, together with R5 and R6, revocation on a 2FA change and no surviving password-only session, given the order of Better Auth's writes: flag, replacement session, then `verified`). Mitigation: the stop rule in Task 31 31.C: the builder stops PR 4 and brings Charlie the evidence and a spec revision proposal; nothing weaker is built before Charlie decides and the revision is reviewed and approved. Cost: PR 4, and the PRs after it (the order is sequential), wait for that decision.
18. **Phase 0 sessions at the Phase 1 cutover (Bob plan review Round 9, P1).** Phase 0 stores live sessions with no audit row; without a cutover rule the first upgraded database breaks R2 at once. Mitigation (Task 31 R15 → T15, builder proposal, open point G): the migration that installs the audit mechanism locks `session`, deletes every row and only then installs the mechanism, writing no audit row. Cost: every user signs in again once after the upgrade (Phase 0 has only test data; the dev volume is reset anyway, Risk 5; a production upgrade from Phase 0 announces it in the upgrade notes and runbook). Residuals: a session created by a Phase 0 process that is still running during the migration waits on the lock and is then either deleted or audited (T15 concurrent-writer case); a Phase 0 cookie for a deleted session is simply refused (no session cookie cache is configured, asserted in T15). The `pre_existing` alternative (keep and record the sessions) is rejected for now; it would add an event category and an E12 change.
19. **Recovery-code revocation scope is a policy choice (Bob plan review Round 9, P2).** R5 makes regeneration end other sessions and a recovery-code sign-in end none (open point F). If Bob or Charlie rules that consumption must revoke too, R5, T5 and E12 change together before the spike. The candidate design cannot make the regeneration case work from the database alone (the trigger cannot tell regeneration from consumption, 31.E), so the spike adds a path-aware step, and T5 (c2) checks that new codes and the revocation commit together or not at all.

The mechanism is chosen in the PR 4 spike (the candidate, revocation in the flag statement plus a sign-in re-check, 31.E, is what Round 8 showed causes the lockout). Tests: T5, T6, T11 (PR 4); Task 24 Step 2 end to end through `next start`, including the concurrent seam and an interrupted enrolment (PR 7). **Password change:** Task 24 Step 2 checks that a second session is refused after it; if 1.7.6 as configured does not revoke, the builder stops and raises it with Bob and Charlie before PR 7. Email change is not enabled. Each rotation's new session is audited (R2). Tests for the 2FA scope: T5 (a)–(c2) and its negative consumption case. |

Every session row has its audit row, written atomically with it (Task 31 R2 → T2 and the session invariant), also on a database upgraded from Phase 0: every Phase 0 session is invalidated in the migration that installs the mechanism and no login row is fabricated (R15 → T15, Bob plan review Round 9), and `/list-sessions` returns no tokens (R10 → T10). Owner scope choice B (Charlie, 2026-10-21): this plan fixes the requirements R1–R15 (31.A) and the required tests T1–T15 (31.B); the mechanism is chosen in the PR 4 design spike (31.C), built against the installed Better Auth and reviewed by Bob as code before the rest of PR 4. Requirement level: one row per login and 2FA event, no workspace (R1); no session without its row, atomically (R2); exactly one login row per login (R3); 2FA changes atomic with their row (R4); the three 2FA changes (enable, disable, recovery-code regeneration) end other sessions and a recovery-code sign-in does not (R5, AUTH-07); no password-only session after a completed enable, concurrent sign-in included (R6); the reason never disagrees with the result (R7); trusted devices off, old cookies cannot skip the challenge (R8, R9); no tokens from `/list-sessions` (R10); no enrolment lockout (R11, Bob Round 8); append-only, system module (R12); no PII (R13); no new privilege (R14); Phase 0 sessions invalidated at cutover, no fabricated row (R15, Bob Round 9). Not claimed in this plan: that any candidate mechanism (31.E) meets them. Open for Bob: open points A–G of 31.D (R11 recovery policy, completeness of R1–R15, the password-step residual, event vocabulary, retention and erasure, recovery-code revocation scope, Phase 0 session cutover). If the spike shows a requirement cannot be met as written, the builder stops and brings Charlie a spec revision proposal (31.C stop rule; risk 17). |

Requirements: each row is written atomically with what it records (no session is stored without its row, and no 2FA change commits without its row); a completed login has exactly one sign-in row; enabling 2FA, disabling 2FA and regenerating the recovery codes each end the user's other sessions, while signing in with a recovery code ends none (it is a login, recorded as one together with its recovery-code change), and no session obtained without the second factor survives a completed 2FA enable, also when a sign-in runs at the same time; when a database from before Phase 1 is upgraded, every session that existed before is ended in the migration that starts this audit, before it starts, and no audit row is invented for it, so every user signs in again once; no failure or interruption at any step of 2FA enrolment leaves the user locked out (the user can complete or restart enrolment without an operator); trusted devices are off, and a trusted-device cookie issued before Phase 1 cannot skip the 2FA step; the session list returns no tokens.   - **(d) "Audit, logging, privacy", line 835.** After "…purges, member changes." insert: "Logins and 2FA events (each session issued, with the reason: sign-in, the password step of a 2FA sign-in, or a rotation; the 2FA step of a sign-in; 2FA enabled; 2FA disabled; recovery codes changed) belong to a user, not a workspace, and are written to `auth_audit_log` (no RLS, system module only, append-only; one row per event, also for a user with no workspace; revision 8, ASK 3 = B, Charlie 2026-10-20); every other event above is written to `audit_log` in its workspace. Each `auth_audit_log` row is written atomically with what it records: no 2FA change commits without its row, so a failed audit write leaves 2FA as it was; no session exists without its row, so a sign-in whose row cannot be written fails with no session and no token, and an interrupted sign-in leaves neither. Each completed login has exactly one sign-in row, also when 2FA is enabled or disabled while the sign-in runs. Enabling 2FA, disabling 2FA and regenerating the recovery codes each end every other session of the user; signing in with a recovery code ends none (it is a login, recorded as one together with its recovery-code change). No session obtained without the second factor survives a completed 2FA enable, also when a sign-in runs at the same time. When a database from before Phase 1 is upgraded, every session that existed before is ended in the migration that starts this audit, before the audit starts, and no audit row is invented for it: every user signs in again once, and no session exists without its row from the first request. No failure or interruption at any step of 2FA enrolment leaves the user locked out: the user can complete or restart enrolment without an operator. Trusted devices are not offered in Phase 1, and a trusted-device cookie issued before it cannot skip the 2FA step. The session list returns no session tokens. How this is achieved is a Phase 1 implementation choice, recorded in `docs/DECISIONS.md`." *(These are Task 31 requirements R1–R11 and R15 (owner scope choice B, 2026-10-21; R5 scope and R15 after Bob plan review Round 9); the mechanism is deliberately not in the spec. Bob's rulings on Task 31 open points A–G may change the wording before his final round.)*
